mr president, i would like to thank you. mr president, i welcome your presidency. the actual entry into force of the euro is a celebration for those of us who want european cohesion and economic development. the task of the eu is now to ensure that the euro lives up to the expectations placed on it. that is why the spanish priority of a dynamic and competitive europe is so urgent. the socialist leader here in the house, who seems to be rather uninterested in this debate, was very 'kind' in his comment on this. in fact, mr aznar, as prime minister you have made possible a much more dynamic spain thanks to the modernisation of the administration and the economy. through major structural changes and tax cuts, spain has reduced the barriers to new business and created new jobs. as a result, unemployment is falling and prosperity is rising in spain. it may be sad for baron crespo, but it's good for the spanish. this is precisely the transformation that the eu must undertake if it is to be serious about the lisbon process. in ten years, the socialists have only let two pass. the spanish presidency must now step up the pressure and not just talk but also pursue a policy of prosperity, development and opportunity for the people of europe. the enlargement is now in a crucial phase. i trust that spain, which has experienced dictatorship and which has secured democracy and freedom through european cooperation, will now guarantee that the council will not neglect anything in the enlargement work under your leadership, and that spain will show the same solidarity and goodwill as your country has shown for the last fifteen years. finally, i also look forward to a presidency which will meet the united states with friendship, respect and independence. good luck with your endeavors.
the committee on the environment, public health and consumer protection has tabled an opinion on the proposal for a council directive amending directive 76/769/eec on the approximation of the laws of the member states relating to the labelling, presentation and advertising of foodstuffs. mr president, i would like to thank you. there are many ways to protect the environment. here in the european parliament we try to do this in two main ways. we either try to prohibit or reduce environmentally harmful activities by means of directives and regulations, or we try to encourage individuals, companies and public authorities to take voluntary action to reduce environmental disturbances by means of directives and regulations. i would like to say to mr zappala that the time is simply past when we could look at economic criteria alone, and that the ec treaty actually now obliges us to take social and environmental considerations into account alongside economic considerations. the two proposals for directives on public procurement must therefore enable local and regional authorities and public authorities to procure goods and services in such a way as to minimise the impact on the environment. i think the commission's proposal is strange in that it makes such environmentally friendly procurement impossible. in the two opinions which i have drawn up for the committee on the environment, public health and consumer affairs, we have clearly included the possibility of taking environmental considerations into account in public procurement. these opinions were adopted in their entirety without any amendments, which is a very strong and clear sign from the committee on the environment. i am also pleased to note that the majority of the amendments tabled by the committee on the environment have been adopted by the committee responsible. there are, however, a few points where the committee responsible has achieved remarkable results. in particular, the proposal for a directive on public procurement of goods, services and works, in which article 53 must be amended to make it much easier to take account of the environment in public procurement, while the second of the two directives, namely the proposal for a directive on public procurement in the fields of water, energy and transport, does not make the same amendment to article 54, which is identical to article 53 of the first directive. i suspect that this is an oversight, but i would like to emphasize this and call on everyone to vote in favour of amendment no 140 to the report on public procurement of goods, services and works. these changes are significant. as i said, the two directives should be consistent. if the wording 'the most economically advantageous tender for the contracting authority' is retained, this means that the contracting authority does not have to take environmental considerations or consider the public interest or the life cycle in a broader perspective. it is therefore very important that we actually change this to conform to the wording of the proposal for a directive on public procurement of goods, services and works. these criteria are included in the sixth environmental programme. they are part of the integrated product policy, and the rules are also intended to promote sustainable development in accordance with the treaty of amsterdam, to give just a few examples. i cannot understand the commission's incomprehensible deterioration of the existing directive, since the commission believes that it is not permissible to take, for example, a life cycle perspective as an evaluation criterion. however, legal proceedings have been initiated in this respect. the advocate general has apparently stated that the view of the committee on the environment and myself is the correct one and not the one the commission is advocating. i am also very pleased with the amendments made by the committee on employment and social affairs, because sustainable development means taking account of economic, social and environmental criteria. i therefore hope that we will vote in favour of the amendments tabled by the green group and the pse group.
mr president, i would like to thank you. these two directives are important in creating a well-functioning internal market. public procurement in the eu amounts to eur 1.5 billion per year, of which eur 40 million is spent in sweden alone. the figures show the importance of clear and simple rules of the game. smes must be able to compete on equal terms. if the eu is to become the most competitive market in the world by 2010, it is necessary to stimulate and facilitate the conditions for smes. it is important to maintain the thresholds in order to achieve solutions that benefit consumers and stimulate competition. this will help to increase the number of contracts and create the conditions for a european market in the field of public procurement. it prevents protectionist behaviour on national markets and makes it easier for politicians to be frugal with the taxpayers' money, which is so important! clear rules must also apply to environmental and social considerations. the two directives we are dealing with today are economic directives. where and when environmental and social factors are to be taken into account, this should be done when establishing the criteria for the procurement. this will make the rules clear to those who submit tenders and will enable them to know the conditions of the contract. the contract shall be awarded to the tenderer with the most economically advantageous tender. i also want to agree entirely with what mr thors said about his experience of small countries versus the german market. i have the same experience with my country. however, i also want to fully acknowledge the huge lack of competition in my own country in the building sector, which also leads to high housing costs. our aim should be to simplify, streamline and increase crossborder procurement in order to improve quality and reduce costs.
mr president, i would like to thank you. i would like to thank mr myller for having had this action programme as his task for a long time and for having fought hard to get it right. it is good to know that we are finally at the stage where we can take a position on the sixth environmental action programme. in view of this, it is perhaps not so surprising that many of us wish, and would have wished, that the programme had been better, and feel disappointment that it did not turn out as well as we had hoped. at the same time, we may also feel some frustration at the unfair distribution of power that we sometimes see between the institutions and we want to demonstrate this in some way, particularly in the case of the sixth environmental action programme. then i would hope that liberals and others who think along these lines would take a stand and put their own frustrations behind them. let us separate policy from process, let us do what we are mandated to do, let us do it well and take the institutional and constitutional battle elsewhere, not in the context of the sixth environmental action programme. look at the sixth environmental action programme! we shall use it as a guiding light for the whole of the union's policy, for environmental policy but also in other areas, as a foundation for the whole of the sustainable development strategy which we shall be taking to johannesburg in september this year. there is no reason to reject this document. we have to adopt it today. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of biofuels. i would like to mention what has recently been reported in sweden. it says that the sharp decline in the amount of climate-changing gases is due precisely to the carbon tax. it is further evidence that we must continue the fight with the commission to ensure that the council is convinced that it is now time to have a common environmental taxation, not least in the area of carbon dioxide.
mr president, i would like to thank you. in dealing with environmental issues and with the various measures, programmes, legislation, conventions, etc., one is often surprised and impatient at the slowness and difficulty of formulating and implementing environmental and health protection objectives. this is also the case with the sixth environmental action programme. at first, it was not certain that we would get any action programme at all. when we then saw the commission's proposal, we thought it was very vague and lacked both qualitative and quantitative targets and timetables. of course, an environmental action programme must set environmental objectives if there is to be any point in having a programme at all. the rapporteur, mr myller, and the committee on the environment have really tried to improve the sixth environmental action programme. i would like to thank the rapporteur for this and also thank other colleagues for the good cooperation. in the second reading, we will now consider the council's position, and we will note that the council has indeed tabled a very large number of parliament's amendments. however, the process of specifying times, etc., is still very slow. we must now take the opportunity to change this by voting in favour of the committee's amendments and the compromise amendments put forward by the various groups. the overall objective of the programme is to break the link between environmental stress and economic growth. so far, the improvements made have been eaten up by increased production and consumption. another overarching principle is to provide the basis and priority for sustainable development and to apply the precautionary principle and substitution. in order to make this possible, i would like to recommend to all members to vote in favour of amendment no 44, which says that by 2003 we must find out which eu aid is going in the opposite direction, i.e. has a negative impact on the environment, and determine the year in which it must be abolished. as regards chemicals and pesticides, i think all the amendments tabled on this are good. the umbrella organisation of chemical organisations cefic has commented that the amendments are not in line with the chemicals strategy report. i'd argue that it's completely out of the blue. it is sad that such an incomplete and one-sided analysis has been made. as rapporteur for the white paper, i can assure you that these amendments are good and that they strengthen the sixth environmental programme. as for the eldr group's proposal, we would have liked, as did hulthén, to have had a stronger programme and to have had more success. however, we have now the proposal we have. there is so much we have to do and agree on within these institutions. i would therefore strongly reject the liberal proposal and call on everyone to vote for the environment committee's proposal and for the compromise amendments which have been tabled.
mr president, i would like to thank you. i shall first touch on the proposal from the eldr group to reject the common position. that is a proposal which we cannot support. we think it would be irresponsible not to take a decision on the sixth environmental action programme. i too would have liked the proposal to go much further. the original proposal was very weak and vague. in fact, large number of new countries. it also risks causing some problems in environmental work. environmental work risks losing momentum and it may be more difficult to push through new environmental laws. mr president, i would like to thank you. i shall not be too critical but will try to look ahead to a forthcoming white paper rather than discuss the somewhat strange green paper proposal. in the food safety sector, which is where i work most, after a series of scandals and disasters, they have finally come close to a 'soil-to-table' principle which is very effective when trying to legislate and trying to see the context. i would like the white paper to be based on the same principle, so that we can follow a line for each product and see it in its context: raw materials, final or renewable, energy use, what energy is used, chemicals, product flow schemes, logistics, etc. this is particularly important for the very large volumes of products, which are actually food, water and household chemicals. they account for more than half of our road transport and consume a tremendous amount of resources, even though we may not even consider them to be products. i therefore call for a strategy where everything is traceable, where we can follow the product backwards, and principles that are valid whether it is a car or a package of sausages for lunch.
in writing. - (sv) what is it? we, the swedish social democrats, jan andersson, anneli hulthén, göran färm and hans karlsson, voted against amendment no 63 because we think it is important that framework agreements can also be concluded for intellectual services. the term 'spiritual services' should be clarified. in sweden, framework agreements are very common in the fields of architecture, medicine, accounting, banking and consultancy services. the adoption of this amendment would have adverse consequences for sweden. we in the swedish social democrats voted in favour of amendment no 16, which we consider to be reasonable and balanced. it is right that public tenders should not be awarded to market players who have been involved in criminal organisations or are responsible for corruption or fraud. however, we consider it important to mention in the recital that it is for the member states to determine the conditions which must apply and the penalties which must have been imposed in order for a conviction to entail exclusion from a public tender. we also voted against amendments nos 48, 79 to 83, 86 to 90, but we would like to stress that we do not think that a list of these offences should be included in the directive, as such a list can never be complete.

- the next item is the report (a5-0444/2001) by mrs cerdeira morterero, on behalf of the committee on civil liberties, justice and home affairs, on an initiative of the united kingdom, the french republic and the kingdom of sweden with a view to the adoption by the council of a framework decision on the application of the principle of mutual recognition to financial penalties (a5-0444/2001) (com (2001) 0178 - c5-0443/2001 - 2001/0825 (cns)).
commission. - madam president, ladies and gentlemen, first of all i would also like to extend my warmest congratulations to mrs charlotte cederschiöld on her election as vice-president of this house. the commission welcomes this initiative, which is under discussion today, by the united kingdom, the french republic and the kingdom of sweden to ensure mutual recognition of financial penalties. this issue was addressed - it should be recalled - in the commission communication on the mutual recognition of final decisions in criminal matters which we published and discussed here in the european parliament in july 2000. the need for this specific instrument was identified in the programme of measures to implement the principle of mutual recognition of criminal decisions adopted by the council and the commission in november 2000. i would like to thank mrs carmen morterero in particular for her excellent report. as you have rightly pointed out, there is no international instrument currently in force in all our member states concerning the application of financial penalties in criminal matters. there are only the two instruments to which the honourable member refers, but one has never entered into force and the other has only been ratified by five member states. this means that we are now faced with the first european union instrument in this field, which i think is also ambitious. the instrument implements the principle of mutual recognition for the purposes of cross-border enforcement of financial penalties, thereby ensuring that there are no safe havens within the union for those who attempt to evade the application of financial penalties imposed by the competent authorities of the member states. this instrument builds on the progress made in implementing the principle of mutual recognition through the council's approval of the commission's proposal on the european arrest warrant. as mrs ludford has already mentioned, the commission is working on an initiative to identify the principles and essential criteria for the legal protection of citizens' fundamental rights in criminal matters. but it is also worth remembering that we are not dealing with a blank sheet of paper here: we are working within an existing legal framework which is, i might say, consolidated. all member states have signed and acceded to the european convention on human rights, all member states have legal systems of criminal prosecution which are subject to the jurisdiction of the court of justice of human rights, based in strasbourg, and all member states have jointly proclaimed, from a political, not a legal point of view, the charter of fundamental rights of the european union. these are legal and political instruments which enable us to establish the framework of reference for the application of the principle of mutual recognition of final decisions in criminal matters. in general, the commission shares the approach followed in all the amendments proposed by the rapporteur. we understand the rationale of the proposal to set a minimum threshold of eur 40 for the implementation of the principle in that in some cases below eur 40 the costs of transfer and implementation would not justify the implementation of the decision. i fully understand the criterion of the 40 euro, which is imported, so to speak, from the schengen agreement. i would just like to stress that the wording should be sufficiently careful not to give the impression that we are allowing the mere fact of crossing a border to be a way of escaping criminal liability, even if it is very limited in terms of money. finally, i would like to thank mrs carmen morderero once again for the positive and innovative spirit of her report and to express my hope that it will be adopted by the european parliament.
- thank you, chief inspector vitorino. the joint discussion is closed. the vote will take place at 5 p.m.
- the next item is the report (a5-0450/2001) by mrs kauppi, on behalf of the committee on employment and social affairs, on the communication from the commission to the council and the european parliament on the training and recruitment of seafarers (com (2001) 188 - c5-0468/2001 - 2001/2188 (cos)).
the commission. - madam president, ladies and gentlemen, the communication on the training and recruitment of seafarers which we are discussing today is an important document for the commission, which is intended to stimulate a debate at european level on the worrying problem of the decline in the number of community seafarers. the commission is convinced that the european union in general, and its shipping industry in particular, cannot do without well-trained, efficient and motivated seafarers. 90% of the union's external trade and more than 35% of its internal trade depend on maritime transport, making it a vital activity for the union's economy. the communication explains that the community cannot afford to lose its fundamental store of experience if it does not want to jeopardize safety and the environment, as well as the competitiveness of maritime transport and related sectors. in addition, in the long term, the expected shortage of community seafarers will lead to insufficient staff for the eu's shore-based activities associated with maritime transport. the knowledge and experience acquired by community seafarers during their time at sea must be preserved if the european union is to avoid harming the shipping industry as a whole. the communication makes a number of recommendations. all the proposals are based on the principle that, as long as the maritime career remains socially and financially unattractive compared to work on shore, it will not be able to attract quality seafarers and therefore ensure efficient operations, safe navigation and good ship maintenance, as well as achieving the reduction - which the maritime community needs - in the number of accidents and in marine pollution itself. this is especially true today, as in the light of recent terrorist attacks, even greater attention should be paid to the human factor in all sectors, including the maritime sector. i am particularly pleased to see that the european parliament subscribes to this philosophy and has supported most of our recommendations, while contributing proposals which i consider very constructive. i would therefore like to thank the rapporteur, mrs piia-noora kauppi, and the whole of the european parliament for the way in which they have taken our communication. the report which parliament is going to adopt today will go a long way towards stimulating the political debate on this subject. i hope that the member states will also agree to deal with this matter quickly. in fact, most of the actions proposed by the commission, which i hope will be supported by parliament, will have to be implemented by the member states, the maritime institutes, the maritime sector and the social partners, and we hope to see the wheels turning soon. today we are calling on these bodies to act, and the commission is grateful for that. for our part, we are ready to continue research into the employment, education and training of seafarers and to analyse the economic variables underlying maritime transport, as requested by mrs piia-noora kauppi's report.
- thank you, chief inspector vitorino. the joint discussion is closed. the vote will take place at 5 p.m.

commission. – madam president, i will be brief because i spoke for too long in my first speech. i would like to underline that i spoke on behalf of commissioner bolkestein who is responsible for these issues. i should like to make three points. the first is the influence of the european union in the basle committee. i mentioned the global framework. nine out of 13 members of the basle committee are members of the european union. the commission and the european central bank are involved in its debates. we have the opportunity to convey the european union's points of view in the debates and in the basle committee framework. thanks to the coordination of our positions, we influenced the debates in the basle committee, in the areas of review of the capital requirements applicable to lending to smes and the wider issue of recognition of collateral. those are two examples where our proposals did play a major role in the debates on the basle committee. secondly, creating compatibility between flexibility and transparency is a key issue for the credibility of the overall system. it will be necessary to establish not only the relationship between the minimum capital requirements of the institution and the risks that such an institution takes on board, but also to guarantee that there will be transparency in the criteria that are used by the supervisory bodies of the member states. that is why the financial services action plan is seeking a certain degree of convergence of the supervision in order to guarantee that flexibility in the application of the system will not detract from the necessary transparency and the outside control of the way each system acts. finally, let me ask you to save your more detailed and technical questions for my colleague, mr bolkestein. i am sure he will be willing to give you full and comprehensive answers. i do not dare to go any further than this because let me remind you that the last renaissance man was leibnitz and he died some years ago!

- (sv) mr president, we are discussing two very important proposals for a directive at the moment, which the epp/ed group enthusiastically supports. as regards the directive on mobile road transport operators, it is proposed that this occupational group should be covered by the general legislation on working time. this issue is of course very important for the workers, but it is also important the rapporteur. mr president, mr maystadt, i would like to thank you for your kind words. i would like to thank mr maystadt very much for the readiness and goodwill that you and your colleagues have shown me and the committee. it has made our work much easier. i also want to thank my colleagues. the role of the eib has been a topic of debate in recent years, and criticism has been voiced. it has mainly concerned shortcomings in environmental considerations, openness and transparency. the eib has understood the criticism and substantial changes have taken place. for this, mr maystadt, you and your colleagues deserve our recognition. this year's analysis is in many respects a follow-up to the solid work carried out by lipietz a year ago. some of the views expressed then are now being repeated. the most important issue for the eu is, as we know, enlargement. it is a fundamental change that poses a major challenge for the candidate countries, but also for the current member states. today, most eib loans are granted for various types of regional development projects within the eu. while this is in line with the eib's statute, future enlargement makes it necessary to reconsider this allocation of resources. the share of the future member states in the total amount of loans must be increased. the european union's solidarity must of course also extend to the countries outside the union which have so far benefited from eib loans. this applies to the balkans, the mediterranean and the acp countries. now it's russia's turn to play. in addition, it should be discussed which ldcs the eib has a mandate from the council to support and whether the 49 ldcs, for example, are not in fact in greater need of assistance than the acp countries which the union has traditionally supported. in the information society, economic growth and an improved standard of living are associated not only with new roads and industries but also with education, innovation, new technologies, electronic commerce and the development of other knowledge-intensive sectors. in my view, this should be reflected more fully in the eib's activities. it is important that the eib continues to strengthen its support for smes. there is clear potential for growth in that area. the eib needs to become more active in spreading awareness of the opportunities already available to grant such loans. from being a rather closed institution, the eib has begun to change its attitude towards information and openness. contact with democratically elected representatives at local and regional level is important. knowledge of the projects is at local level. contact with ngos has been improved. the bank's website has also been improved. the eib has published a list of projects in the planning phase. in my view, it would be good if all public documents from the internal evaluation unit were available online. at present, the eib is supervised by the bank's board of governors, i.e. the 15 finance ministers, but there is no supervisory authority. the committee has discussed the possibility of having the european central bank, the ecb, exercise supervision over the eib, as the council can, under the treaty, entrust the ecb with supervisory tasks. if the council does not wish to give this power to the ecb, the matter should be clarified at the next igc, with the aim of introducing specific supervision of the eib's activities. the committee also calls for the bank to be able to provide the necessary financial support to the development of the banking sector. it should be noted that the eib has already shown its willingness to cooperate more closely with olaf. the environment is the issue that most interests the independent organisations. these issues were also given a lot of attention in the lipietz report last year. i believe that the eib has become more aware of environmental issues and that its support for environmental projects has increased substantially. another key factor is the eib's ability to make accurate assessments of environmental issues. at present, the eib has only one person responsible for coordinating environmental issues, while the project teams are composed of engineers and economists who have received additional environmental training. in my opinion, reinforcements are needed here. more trained environmental experts should be employed or contracted to carry out reliable audits of the environmental impact assessments. the requirements for the european investment bank are therefore many. in order to meet all these needs, the bank's capital needs to be further increased. in particular, member states should consider reviewing the ratio of external capital to equity capital to include the eib's reserves, in line with that of commercial banks that can handle higher risks. mr president, i would like to thank you. i would like to welcome the commissioner to the house. finally, i would like to comment on the amendments that have been tabled. i can support amendments nos 1, 3, 4 and 6 by mr lipietz and others. they do not change the content of the report but highlight environmental issues and issues of openness and transparency. i cannot, however, support amendment no 2, which i consider to be unnecessarily far-reaching. amendment no 5 can only be supported as an addition to paragraph 30.
mr president, i would like to thank you. i would like to begin by thanking the rapporteur, my swedish colleague olle schmidt, for a very good report. it highlights the key areas of future european development which the eib has a major responsibility to promote within the framework of its mandate. it also addresses the requirements that parliament should place on the eib as europe's financial institution. i would therefore like to limit myself to highlighting some of the aspects which i consider to be the most important. the commission has also adopted a communication on the future of the european union's external relations. as regards the candidate countries, it is of the utmost importance that their share of the total loan amount of eur 36 billion be significantly increased. in 2000, they received eur 2.9 billion. in view of the forthcoming reunification of the european family, an increase is necessary if the lisbon summit objective is to be achieved. in the case of projects, it is important to safeguard europe's competitiveness and therefore to give priority to infrastructure in the broad sense. this includes communication in the traditional sense, such as roads, interconnections and the possibility of energy-efficient transport systems for goods and people, but also in the sense of telecommunications, it, electronic commerce, support for new technologies, increased knowledge and training. smes account for over 60% of jobs in the eu today. in an enlarged eu, they are likely to cover a much higher percentage. of the 18 million enterprises in the eu, 99% are smes, which employ 66% of the workforce. over 90% of these companies have fewer than 10 employees. if existing smes, like many new businesses, are to be given the opportunity to grow and become larger, or even become large companies, the efforts in the form of information, project involvement and access to risk capital must be strengthened. the rapporteur has proposed that the commission be asked to give the ecb powers to supervise the eib. i think that's good. the bank has no national affiliation and cannot therefore be compared to a private commercial bank. the ecb is therefore an appropriate supervisory body, possibly pending a european financial supervisory authority. in view of the lisbon summit objective, priority must therefore be given to projects that lead to economic growth, development and increased employment in an enlarged europe and that attach great importance to the growth potential of smes. competitiveness and sustainability should be the guiding principles. the requirements on the eib for the information and evaluation of projects must be increased and the importance of proper professional supervision and auditing emphasised.
mr president, i would like to thank you. the initiative for a parliament resolution on the annual accounts of the european investment bank was taken by the verts/ale group, with lipietz as the original rapporteur, as also pointed out by olle schmidt. i think it is necessary for the european parliament to express its opinion on the activities of the eib, since the eib is not just a bank but also an eu institution which finances major projects and manages development with community funds. the eib has been criticised for its neglect of environmental issues and also for its lack of openness and transparency. olle schmidt also mentioned that in response to the criticism, positive changes in eib policy have recently taken place. i hope the eib will continue in the same spirit. i would like to congratulate the rapporteur on an excellent report which addresses important aspects. it is proposed, inter alia, to increase lending to the candidate countries and to make the evaluation more easily accessible to the public. another proposal is to strengthen the dialogue between the social partners, politicians, ngos, etc. as part of openness and transparency. the report could have been stronger on environmental issues. the verts/ale group has tabled six amendments which deal specifically with environmental and transparency aspects. if we are to express our support for an increase in the eib's capital, one condition should be that the projects lead to sustainable development. performance indicators must also be established to enable the eib's activities to be measured and evaluated effectively. i am glad that the rapporteur has expressed his support for most of our amendments. i also accept amendment no 5 as an addition to paragraph 30, as requested by mr olle schmidt. it would be a pity if amendment no 2 were not adopted. if civil society is to follow a case, it is obvious that the texts must be in the national language. i would urge members to vote in favour of this amendment.
mr president, i would like to thank you. the european investment bank is one of the unknown powers of the european union. with a lending volume of eur 36 billion in 2000 and a total capital stock of eur 100 billion, it exercises considerable influence through its policies. despite the bank's influence, there has been very little discussion of it. this is the second year that we have discussed the annual report in parliament. perhaps most remarkable, however, is the lack of discussion among the owners, in the member states and in the parliaments of the various member states, who i believe are in many cases quite unaware of the bank's activities. this can be compared with the world bank, which has been subject to extensive scrutiny and discussion, or with the european development bank, which has also been scrutinized in a different way from the european investment bank. the eib has been the subject of considerable criticism, particularly in two areas. firstly, the eib has been criticised for supporting a number of projects that have increased environmental problems and for shortcomings in environmental impact assessments. secondly, the eib has been criticised for showing a lack of openness and willingness to engage in dialogue, for example with environmental movements in different countries. i think that this criticism has been justified. a comparison, for example, of how the world bank does environmental impact assessments and how they are published, shows that the world bank is actually much better than the european investment bank in that respect. it is not often that a man from the left has reason to praise the world bank, but in this case he does. in recent years, the european investment bank has shown a new attitude towards greater openness, which is very welcome. the important thing is that this new positive attitude is also followed up by concrete action. olle schmidt raises some demands for action in his report, which i agree with, such as the need for more resources, for example more staff working on environmental issues and greater transparency, but it is necessary to go further. i believe, in particular, that it is a reasonable requirement that environmental impact assessments, if they are carried out, should always be published before decisions are taken. eu requirements for environmental impact assessments must also be met when loans are granted for projects outside the european union, and they must be translated into the national languages of the countries receiving the loans. the european investment bank should also follow the aarhus convention, which implies a commitment to high transparency towards the environmental movement. that would be a great improvement. the eib should also have a more developed policy on development issues, i.e. development in the third world, as the bank's activities there increase. the gothenburg summit mandated the eib to support sustainable development and to invest in climate issues. this should have far-reaching implications for the type of projects that can be supported in the transport and energy sectors. this should mean a change of direction on the part of the bank, and hopefully it does. i believe that reforms should be a condition for increasing the bank's capital. only if the bank is obliged to implement these reforms will it be agreed to increase its capital. i am opposed to the idea of the european central bank acting as a supervisory authority. the european central bank is a genuinely undemocratic structure which should not have this kind of task.

the rapporteur. madam president, i would like to thank you. let me begin by thanking all those who have helped with this report colleagues from other political groups, not least roth-behrendt, who was formerly chairman of the temporary committee of inquiry into bse, as well as the commission and the office for food and veterinary affairs in dublin. globalization is increasing day by day. it is good that we are increasingly doing business across borders. we need the free movement of goods within the european union and also the increasingly free movement of goods in the world. it is important, however, that this does not lead to a more free flow of infectious agents. as regards the mad cow disease, the european union seems to have "left the free market in the dust". first, a free market was created, and then we started to look at whether the rules were sufficient for a free market. in recent years, a number of encouraging measures have been taken. for example, we have created the european food authority. in addition, the food legislation has been reviewed and will lead to good legislation once all the acts have entered into force. but the experience scares you! looking back, many member states have been bad at complying with the legislation. so it is not only the lack of legislation but also the lack of compliance that has resulted in the current situation. it is very important that we now remedy this and become better at complying with the legislation. if we look at the links between these different diseases, we can see that scrapie, the oldest tse disease, was discovered hundreds of years ago, while bse, which affects cattle, was not discovered until 1986. it is clear that we are at a stage when a lot is happening. life has not stopped - nor have its negative aspects. we must therefore be prepared to discover new problems all the time. during the autumn there was concern that bse could also attack sheep. that is probably why paragraph 11 on the control of sheep has been inserted in the report. i think this paragraph is incorrect and i would ask members not to vote for it. the study that was supposed to have been carried out on sheep was not on sheep brains but was probably on co-brains. even scientists make mistakes sometimes. the problem with bse is that this disease crosses species barriers. this means that there is a risk of human infection as well; we know that creutzfeldt-jacob disease is a major concern in this context. i would like to point out some of the measures we propose for the future in the report. first of all, i would like to say that, for ethical reasons, meat meal should of course never be fed to ruminants. they are not naturally carnivorous and should not be forced to eat meat. it is, however, reasonable - we will return to this in other reports - that raw materials suitable for human consumption should also be used as feed. the problem is that if an animal eats animals of the same species, there is a risk of the disease occurring. therefore, a so-called cannibalism ban should be introduced, prohibiting the feeding of animals with feed produced from animals of the same species. the risk of infection is high, and compliance has been weak. we must therefore conclude that it will not be possible in the foreseeable future to lift the ban on feeding meat and bone meal to the general public. in the long run, as compliance improves, it may be possible to do so. on the important issue of sanctions, i would like to put a direct question to commissioner byrne, which is as follows: if individual member states do not comply with their obligations under the treaty, the eu must have the power to intervene by taking measures to ensure food safety, which we also address in several points in the report. we also believe that the commission must be able to impose sanctions on member states which fail to apply this type of legislation. it would be particularly interesting to hear the commissioner's views on this. in conclusion, i would like to return to what i actually started with. research is necessary both in this area and in other neighbouring areas, in order to be alert to the emergence of new problems and new diseases. it is extremely important that we in the eu allocate sufficient funds to keep the fight against this type of disease, which is dangerous for both animals and humans, at a high level.
the rapporteur. madam president, i would like to thank you. first of all, i'd like to thank commissioner byrne. i am very pleased that he has answered so clearly to my direct question on sanctions, and that this is a matter under consideration in the commission. i look forward to hearing from this house the commission's proposal on how this should be dealt with. i regard this as a major step forward both in this respect and in food control in general. i can also tell the commissioner that i share his view on the proposal to lower the age of animals to be tested for bse to 24 months. it wasn't my idea. it came from somewhere else. i will therefore help to ensure that this proposal is voted down tomorrow, using the same arguments as the commissioner. i would like to take this opportunity to thank my colleagues for all the kind words. it has been a pleasure to work on this report. however, i would like to conclude by stressing that the danger is not over. as we saw in 2001, new cases have been detected in many new countries, not only in the eu but also in the candidate countries slovenia and slovakia, as well as in japan. bse is still a problem. i share the view expressed by mr whitehead on the bse study which was found to have been carried out on cowhide brains instead of sheep brains. it means that the only thing we know in many of these situations is that we really don't know anything, or we don't know enough. that's why we need research.

in writing. - i voted against the report. the reason for this is that the legal basis for this report is article 95 of the treaty, which provides for a majority decision in the council with the european parliament's co-decision. article 95 states that this rule does not apply to 'tax provisions'. it is therefore a totally decisive step from the point of view of the principles, a kind of new regulation applicable to the treatment of tax matters, which would emerge from interstate cooperation, with the rule of unanimity, according to article 93 of the treaty. i cannot support the commission's view that the report is only about administrative cooperation and has no fiscal implications, because it does not directly concern tax collection. the council has always rejected this view in the past and has changed the legal basis of articles 93 and 94, which only require consultation of parliament. the swedish christian democrats fully support the council's line. we believe that decisions on taxation should not be taken by majority vote in the council with the co-decision of parliament. our position is clear: we believe that measures in this area must remain subject to unanimity, as provided for in article 93 of the treaty.

mr president, commissioner, i would like to thank you for your reply. i would like to begin by thanking the rapporteur, mr hatzidakis, for the very open and compromise-oriented way in which he has worked on this report. the transport of both goods and people by road is increasing. the traffic is international and cross-border. that is why the rules we lay down must be so. the legislation on speed limiters is a shining example. when it came into force in 1994, there had been some resistance from both the industry and the drivers themselves, but this was overcome when the benefits were realised. in a europe where more than 40 000 people die in road accidents, many means must be tried to make traffic safer. after the introduction of cruise control, it was no longer possible to put acceleration into systems for the heaviest vehicles. this new proposal extends the use of cruise control to light trucks and buses, thus increasing the positive impact. the frequency of accidents involving light trucks has increased markedly in recent years. we in the pse group support the amendments for a longer transitional period. however, a question which has come up again in the debate on this directive, and which was raised by mr jarzembowski, is whether different speeds should be allowed for different types of vehicle. the argument was that similarities create congestion and road bottlenecks. i don't think this is right. the less variation in speed, the smoother the traffic actually flows. there are mathematical calculations to prove this. the simplest way is to imagine a road where the vehicles keep the same speed. it'll make the traffic flow more smoothly. i therefore call for amendment no 12 to be rejected.
mr president, i would like to thank you. i have great respect for the ambitious work that the rapporteur, mr hermange, has put into the report on the scoreboard for the implementation of the social agenda. i am, however, concerned that the work is leading to a call for a long list of legislative initiatives from the commission. this applies, among other things, to lifelong learning, work organisation, the need to reconcile work and private life, health and safety policy, supplementary health insurance and the recruitment of staff. there are two main reasons why i believe that demanding legislative initiatives in these areas is the wrong way to go. firstly, we swedish moderates believe that these issues should not be at community level. they are best handled at national level and, of course, best handled through negotiations at national level. secondly, i believe that the possibility of enlargement of the union is being seriously hampered by the addition of more and more pages of legislation to the thousands and thousands of pages of legislation which the applicant countries are already required to accept and implement. i therefore agree with what mr smet said about the importance of stopping, reflecting and reviewing the legislation that already exists. we are not given the opportunity to vote against individual points in the report. we swedish moderates will not be able to support the report as a whole in the final vote, even though there is actually a lot in the report that we think is positive. unfortunately, as i said earlier, the initiatives are not at the right level. they should be on a national level.
in writing. - (sv) what is it? we voted against amendment no 21 on paragraph 3 of the report because we agree with the rapporteur that the european parliament, as well as the national parliaments, in their respective spheres, represent the citizens of the union. on the other hand, we do not believe that the increase in the powers of the parliaments of the union requires that the european parliament's powers be extended for all decisions taken by the union. it is important to remember that transnational cooperation is carried out through governments, which are controlled by national parliaments, which also require more responsibility. we voted against paragraph 7 of the report, because we agree in principle with the rapporteur's view that it would be unfortunate for governments to develop more and more new instruments of power which exclude parliamentary intervention. at the same time, we would like to emphasise that the open method of coordination - despite its shortcomings in terms of the role of parliaments - has been and is an effective and welcome method for increasing integration, cooperation and achieving results in areas which are not considered conducive to decision-making at a more transnational level. we voted against paragraph 8 of the report, because we agree with the rapporteur that dual mandates in the european parliament and in a national/regional parliament cannot be considered legitimate - either in terms of representation or in terms of practical consequences. as regards the term of office in regional parliaments, however, we consider that the rapporteur is referring to parliaments which have autonomous legislative powers and not to parliaments which have been given decision-making powers, such as the county councils in sweden. in the latter case, it would be reasonable to make their tenure compatible with that of the european parliament.

madam president, i would like to thank you. i would first like to congratulate mr cederschiöld on being the first swede to become vice-president of the european parliament. i want to express my pleasure at this election. what happened on december 13 last year in the indian parliament was something extraordinary: the parliament of a democratic, pluralistic country is under attack by militant islamist groups. parliament, the very heart of democracy, is being brutally attacked. people are being murdered. the whole of democracy is being challenged by these militant islamists. it's important that we ourselves try to understand emotionally what happened in new delhi that day. what if our own parliament here in strasbourg or our national parliaments, in my case the riksdag in stockholm, were to be attacked by terrorists? our reactions would be extremely strong. it may be difficult for us europeans to grasp the seriousness of what has happened. i would like to speak to commissioner diamantopoulou about the islamic religious schools, the so-called madrassas, in pakistan. i believe that the commissioner and the european union have a very important task in promoting an education in these schools which is based on the idea of love, which is based on love of neighbour and which does not look at other religions or other political views with the eyes of hatred. it is in these contexts, in these madrasas and in other islamist contexts, that hatred is spread. my dear friends! the taliban regime in afghanistan has fallen, but the taliban regime's mentality of hatred, of violence, of calling other people "rogue" and seeing them as inferior - that mood remains. i would like to call on mr diamantopoulou to do everything possible to ensure that the idea of love, neighbourliness and tolerance is a feature of education in pakistan and, of course, in schools in the middle east. the essence of religion should be to refine the soul, to promote high ethics and morals, to give man spiritual values - not to be in the service of violence and hatred. pakistan served as a training ground for al qaeda terrorists. it was one of three countries in the world that recognized the taliban regime. the taliban ambassador to afghanistan was allowed to remain in pakistan after the september 11 attacks on the united states. pakistan has a lot to deal with. what president musharraf has said is important. now we are waiting for him to act in a powerful way. (applause) i would like to thank you.
thank you for your kind words about the election of a swedish vice president. this election means a lot to the swedish people.
the commission. - mr president, i too would like to congratulate you and wish you success in your mandate. the initiative of parliament is welcomed. the members of parliament have underlined that what has happened is an extremely large terrorist attack against the supreme institution of the world's largest democracy. the european union has unanimously expressed its outrage at this event. the council issued statements condemning the events and expressing the european union's solidarity with the republic of india. the council and the commission have also repeatedly stressed that pakistan must continue and expand its policy against terrorism. india calls on pakistan to demonstrate by concrete action its determination to eliminate all forms of terrorism, a perfectly legitimate demand. after the attack on december 13, we have witnessed an unprecedented deployment of military forces on the line of control on the border between the two countries and there is concern about the increased tension and military clashes. in this context, we welcome the speech of the president of pakistan, mr musharraf, on 12 january, in which he announced decisive measures to combat terrorism and religious fanaticism. it's a step in the right direction and normalizing tensions between pakistan and india. the commission will continue to draw the attention of both sides to the need for cooperation and not the option of a military solution. the european union and india will meet in madrid on 14 february at ministerial level, and the troika's message will have two elements. the european union is fully supporting india in the fight against terrorism, as agreed in the declaration against terrorism at the second summit, and encourages both sides to resume dialogue as soon as possible and to begin withdrawing military forces.

commission. – i should like firstly to respond to mrs maes' comment by referring to an ancient greek saying: "ενός κακού μύρια έπονται". that roughly means "one bad thing can easily lead to many others". i am afraid this is the case in congo. the goma office of the commission's humanitarian aid office was directly in the path of the lava flow. but a four-man team of echo experts, including disaster response and water specialists, was on the ground to assess needs within 36 hours of the eruption. echo flight, the commission's humanitarian air service based in nairobi, has been made available, bringing in the united nations disaster assessment team, volcano experts and other key personnel. joint assessments conducted by echo and the agencies on the ground resulted in the announcement of an emergency decision on sunday, 20 january. eur five million is covering the immediate needs of 100,000 people. that is about one-third of the affected population. operations are focusing on four main areas: water and health, shelter and non-food items, food, and coordination and logistics. the head of echo's africa unit visited the region from 30 january to 1 february. the mission confirmed that urgent needs were being met and that effective coordination was in place. what are our next steps? first, the immediate emergency is now being managed by the agencies and resources already available. further in-kind donations and the introduction of new agencies should be coordinated with the agencies already on the ground, to avoid potential duplication. after careful appraisal of the options, efforts should now begin to focus on balanced, medium-term assistance with resettlement of the homeless and the rehabilitation of essential social infrastructure. finally, concerning mr sylla's main point, the high media profile of this disaster should not distract our attention from the scale of the humanitarian crisis caused by five years' conflict in congo, already echo's biggest customer – eur 32 million in 2002. the commission would stress the need for an early and peaceful solution to the conflict through the implementation of the lusaka agreements and the inter-congolese dialogue. (applause)
i thank you, madam commissioner diamantopoulou. the debate is closed. the vote will take place at 6.30 p.m.

madam president, i would like to thank you. we've all seen the pictures from guantanamo bay of the prisoners in orange jumpsuits. the issue of how they are treated has raised international concern. we have protested in the european parliament, and we are not alone in this. most countries - except for one, cuba, where the base is - have actually protested. the lack of protest in cuba may be due to the fact that people are happy that the focus has shifted from the treatment of prisoners, but this is perhaps not the subject of discussion at the moment. there is uncertainty about the legal status of the prisoners at guantanamo bay, and this needs to be clarified by a competent court. it could be established by a un security council resolution. whatever crimes and atrocities the prisoners at x-ray camp have committed - and we strongly condemn them - they must be dealt with in accordance with international humanitarian norms and principles. until their legal status has been established, they must be considered prisoners of war. the liberal group chairman, graham watson, said yesterday in the house that we are strongly in favour of a revision of the geneva convention. it would be good if we could have newer and more modern rules that could be applied to terrorist acts and prisoners in situations where, as napolitano said, there is no declaration of war. it would be good if the whole of parliament could back this. this parliament supports the united states and the international fight against terrorism. we have shown that very clearly by our vote earlier today. but when democracies wage such a struggle, it must be conducted with respect for human rights and the rule of law. i therefore share the concern expressed by my colleagues in relation to the military courts. it is not acceptable that a taliban, an american taliban, john walker, should be tried in a civilian court when others are to be tried in military courts. we assume that there are common rules, that everyone is treated equally with the ordinary legal certainty and legal protection in civil courts. however, much of this debate could have been avoided if the united states had ratified the protocol on the international criminal court, so that there would have been a permanent court where those who have committed these kinds of acts could also be brought to justice under international rules. i would urge the united states to ratify that protocol. (applause) i would like to thank you.
