- the joint debate on the report (a5-0159/2002) by mr howitt, on behalf of the committee on employment and social affairs, on the commission green paper: promoting a european framework for corporate social responsibility (com (2002) 366 - c5-0161/2002 - 2002/2069 (cos)) is scheduled.
the commission. - madam president, i should first like to thank parliament and the rapporteur, mr howitt, for their great support and their very effective cooperation in the preparation of this communication. the participation in the consultation, as already mentioned, was large, we had more than 250 participants and i think it is interesting to refer to the substance of the agreement that exists from the whole of this consultation. this confirms that there is a consensus that corporate social responsibility is a concept whereby businesses incorporate their social and environmental concerns on a voluntary basis. they are, of course, incorporating them in a way that goes beyond existing legal requirements. the motivation for this function of enterprises cannot of course be contrary to the rules of economics. this is why it is extremely important to make clear and public that there is a link between the implementation of csr programmes by companies and their profits or their acceptance by consumers. in this context, i would say that as far as the debate on whether it should be voluntary or whether it should be legislation is concerned, at least at this time when coordination at european level is beginning, we could say that it should indeed be voluntary, but that initiatives and efforts should be taken so that the final result, that is, the greater involvement of businesses, is as if there were legislation. what does that mean ? it means that at both european and national level there is a need for public authorities and planning in the area of corporate social responsibility, which concerns, firstly, information - and by saying information i think it is very important to make known how much the profits of some companies that have a consistent and continuous participation in social responsibility programmes have increased - secondly, the coordination of this effort so as to have the greatest possible economic and social effects, coordination which should also concern small and medium-sized enterprises, transparency, one of the most important issues raised by the rapporteurs, the possibility of verifying the declarations made by enterprises and the creation of structures for assessing the presence of enterprises. it is one of the issues on which there are different approaches and opinions - and the commission will present its final proposal in july - and, of course, compatibility with existing international agreements, such as the guidelines of the oecd or the international labour organisation, so that there is also cooperation with third world countries. i would like to refer to some very specific points you made in the report by parliament. i would like to reiterate that, as is clear from your text, transparency and the ability to measure and compare the social and environmental performance of companies are absolutely essential and it must be said that in some countries companies are already required to provide some form of information on their social and environmental impact. these legal requirements in some member states can be a powerful tool for increasing transparency and we can see in a ................. procedure between member states what the positives of this approach are. i would of course agree with mrs cratza that the issue of equal opportunities, both in the internal and external policy of enterprises, is one of the key points. i intend to propose the creation of a european stakeholder forum on corporate social responsibility, which will create a platform at european level where all stakeholders will be able to deal with the whole range of practices and instruments of corporate social responsibility and will of course also contribute to what we have said, namely information and coordination both within the european union and at international level. this forum could also deal with the question of the need for a mediator mentioned in your point 51. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos is a very good one. i intend to propose to the commission that csr be integrated into european union policies, but also into commission matters, to ensure that the development of csr is supported. in paragraph 30 i agree with the role of public administrations as i described them earlier, in paragraph 36 on the need for special cooperation with third countries there is a whole dimension in our proposal concerning the role of european enterprises outside the european union's borders. the social responsibility of companies can obviously be expressed in the employment strategy and we can see in the open method of cooperation how we can show the best results and, of course, the social economy sector has a particular experience of social responsibility. these enterprises have a different core of thinking and interests than other enterprises but can actually help greatly in partnerships and networks in all member countries. finally, in paragraph 53 i will agree with your communication on the issue of basic labour standards, but you know that the commission is already proposing to include basic labour standards in bilateral agreements at european and international level and in all relevant areas of european union policy, namely trade, development, external relations and social affairs. i would like to thank mr howitt and all the members who took part in this difficult debate, and i would like to say that your report was the last and most important contribution to the debate and consultation on the green paper and that my colleague mr liikanen and i are going to announce the final text of the white paper in june, after it has been approved by the commission.

in writing. - (sv) we have voted against the report. we agree with some of the arguments put forward, such as the idea that the common agricultural policy should promote the multifunctionality of agriculture, that environmental criteria should be integrated into all forms of agricultural aid, that such aid should be used to promote small family farms, the refusal to return to national agricultural policies, the dissociation between aid and production, and the promotion of more extensive agriculture. we are opposed to several other parts of the report. the lack of consideration for the interests of developing countries is striking. the opening up of the world market is presented as a negative outlook, and the important decision called 'everything but arms' is called into question. it also states that future reforms will have to be aimed primarily at curbing the decline in the number of jobs in the agricultural sector, which is not our view. we do not accept the principle proposed in the report of less strict application of budgetary discipline. we question the real need for new forms of aid for the production of bananas, rice, vegetable proteins and nuts, and for new aid to producer organizations. the reformed agricultural policy must maintain the european agricultural model and pay farmers fairly for the multifunctional tasks that are part of their work. but it is equally clear that this reform is urgently needed: it must remove the negative effects it may have on developing countries, gradually increase market adaptation and ensure that aid is compatible with sustainable development.
mr president, i would like to thank you. the european parliament is one of the most open parliaments in the world. the chamber is often full, although committee meetings are often more crowded. voting and the preparatory work can be followed online, and hundreds of thousands of visitors to the strasbourg and brussels premises can get a feel for the work. i have seen on several occasions sceptical seventeen-year-olds put on their headphones and were fascinated by the fact that it is possible to debate in different languages. something's going on with these 17-year-olds' attitudes. i think it would be good for the legitimacy of the eu if all schoolchildren had the opportunity to visit us. we should be proud of the transparency, although some improvements can be made, which are also being made. however, once you have lost your fascination with openness, it is not always easy to understand how we work. the votes are endless, with hundreds of amendments. instead of a vital debate of quick replies and counter-quotes in the house, we have a series of long monologues in which we read our pre-written papers as i do today. sometimes the chamber is almost empty, even though interesting issues are being debated. this is partly because we speak different languages. however, we can do more to reform the work of parliament. our president is helping by ensuring that we make policy proposals, and with the corbett report we are taking several very important steps forward. as my colleague mr duff has said, the liberals support corbett's work and efforts to tighten up, focus and great deal of creativity in the use of the rules of procedure. we would like to go further to have more lively debates and to clear more issues from the european parliament's table. in the past, proposals have been made to set up a kind of technical committee. perhaps we can return to that at a later stage. we are also not entirely satisfied with the proposal for the handling of urgent matters and have therefore proposed a compromise to highlight these issues. as mr kaufmann pointed out, we are doing important work on human rights which resonates far beyond the walls of the chamber. we will certainly have occasion to make new changes, to go a step further, not least after enlargement, when both chambers and party groups will probably be more heterogeneous than before. the balance between efficiency and democracy must be found. the main changes needed for the european parliament to function fully democratically, responsibly and with dignity depend on treaty changes, and there is little we can do about them at the moment. it is a question of introducing codecision on more issues, of abolishing the peculiar division of the budget and of travelling between strasbourg and brussels. perhaps we can resolve these issues at the convention. in the meantime, we are taking a big step forward with corbett's work, and i would like to congratulate him on that.
mr president, i would like to thank you. i would like to thank mr figueiredo for a very important and good report. the report is no less important in view of the fact that the objectives and ideas of social integration and social solidarity were highlighted in the context of the lisbon decisions two years ago. however, much has happened since then and the situation has changed. many of the governments that contributed to the lisbon decision no longer exist. almost every month, new political power shifts take place in the governments of the member states. this is disturbing. because, as mr pronk pointed out, responsibility for social security and integration lies with the member states or at lower levels, which requires governments in the member states to prioritise solidarity and social integration. if that is not the case, and i fear it is, then we must expect social mobilization from below. women's movements, immigrant organizations, local workers' movements and others who put social solidarity first must then organize and wage a political struggle. it's not enough to have a good heart or good intentions. we know that there are contradictions and conflicting interests and priorities in political life. the poor and the marginalized are not the top priorities of those in political power. i therefore believe that we in europe are heading towards a very acute political situation with a greater distance than we are used to between those in power and those who have major and difficult social problems. i therefore think it is important that the committee on employment in particular should support mr figueiredo's report. i hope that will be the case tomorrow when we vote on the report.

in writing. - (sv) i regret that parliament has today rejected a proposal to allow six months of parental leave following a birth or adoption, during which the member of the european parliament concerned would have been replaced in accordance with the rules applicable to permanent dismissals. under the current rules, a member may be granted leave but he or she is not entitled to a substitute. the parliamentary group of which he is a member therefore loses one member and the same workload is therefore shared out among fewer members. this is a situation which particularly disadvantages parties from small states with a small number of meps. this is the result of pressure on the member concerned to return to his or her seat as soon as possible, to the detriment of his or her family and children. in this house we often say that it is necessary to be able to reconcile family life and work and to urge governments to take measures in this direction. the same must obviously be true of parliament as a place of work. in the rest of the world of work, young parents have the right to leave: this right must therefore also be given to meps. our regulations must reflect a positive view of the family and children, which is not the case today. the fact that it is democratic is also questionable, since it makes it difficult for people of childbearing age or for those who want to start a family to take up the post of member of the european parliament.
the rapporteur. mr president, i would like to thank you. i have been a member of this house for seven years and i am making my last speech, because i am running for the swedish parliament in the autumn. the fall of the wall and the reunification of europe is the most important issue in my political career. it is good to be able to say, as rapporteur for estonia, that we are finally getting close. estonia, which has completed negotiations on 26 of the 31 chapters, has all the conditions for accession in 2004. estonia is an example of how quickly a country that has emerged from long-term occupation and restored democracy has also managed to reorganise its politics and build a strong market-oriented economy with well-functioning institutions. the estonian economy is performing admirably, with growth forecast to be 4.5 percent this year. the estonian krona has been linked to the d-mark and now to the euro for almost ten years. when estonia joins the eu, i hope it will continue its work towards less bureaucracy and better conditions for business. estonia also has the confidence in the future and the power to change that the union needs. under the swedish presidency, the social democratic government boasted of a timetable for enlargement. but we are not building the eu with plans alone. timetables are a prerequisite for action. it is therefore shameful that sweden is now risking the timetable and making agricultural policy a precondition for enlargement. agricultural policy is undergoing modest but real reforms, and fundamental changes are essential. the council representative pointed out that the countries which are now delaying the enlargement of the agricultural chapter are taking on a great responsibility. nothing could be more important than the eu living up to its promises and the final chapter in the reunification of europe being written in copenhagen this autumn. i would like to congratulate all those who have the privilege of working in the various eu institutions and to thank them for the excellent cooperation they have enjoyed over the years.
the rapporteur. mr president, mr president of the council, mr commissioner! i think we should stop and pinch ourselves in the arm and think about how privileged we are to be working on european issues at the moment. the unification of europe, the integration of the countries of central and eastern europe into the european union, is the historic challenge of our time. sometimes i find this unreal, because for a very long time i was used to the idea that there was a dividing line across europe that would stand. fortunately, it did not. but now i feel that it is taking too long - it has already been many years since the dividing line was lost. as mr brok said, there is no longer any excuse for delaying enlargement. providing its people with nutritious and safe food has always been a major ambition of all states. that is why agricultural and food policy plays such a major role, both in surplus and deficit areas of the world. agricultural policy is a crucial issue in the accession negotiations. the union cannot reasonably accommodate all the requests of the applicant countries. however, we must create a sense of understanding and community in the negotiations within the framework of the eu's long-term economic and political commitments. the general affairs council was unable to reach agreement on direct payments. i would like to make it clear that i do not support the swedish government's position but rather the commission's line on the phasing in of direct aid. in our report we propose in one paragraph that the commission should support and assist romania and bulgaria in their further efforts to join the community. i would therefore like to thank the commissioner for having stated that this would be done at the outset. it's fun when we're in the same place in our endeavors. we will also raise a few points which might help to smooth things over a little bit. it is possible that the commission already has these ideas. as regards the fixing of quotas and volumes, we believe that the situation of the applicant countries during the reference period, their production potential and the development of consumption should be taken into account in the framework of the quotas and volumes proposed by the commission. we also believe that we should not disadvantage those countries which, for various reasons, have subjected their agriculture to a tough market in recent years. in addition, in countries with low levels of direct payments before accession, it should be possible to open the possibility of exceeding this level. i am not saying that this should be applied generally, because it is reasonable not to allow any increase beyond previous levels. there should, however, be a possibility, a space for discussion, of exceeding this level, if the economy allows and the competitive situation is not disturbed. given that the candidate countries are undergoing a major transformation, it would also be appropriate to review the application of the cap over time. the president-in-office of the council is right to say that enlargement is one thing, agricultural policy another. however, there are links and we believe it is urgent that both eu and candidate countries start talking immediately about what a future agricultural policy could look like. we are all aware that it must be changed, and such talks might facilitate approval by the applicant countries. i believe that the eu has a key interest in being able to sell its foodstuffs on a functioning world market, in line with the discussions currently taking place in the world food conference and in line with the ambitions of the wto. it is hoped that this will enable the production potential to be exploited. i would like to make a recommendation to the council and the commission: negotiate with knowledge, emotion and love and we will achieve a good result.
madam president, i would like to thank you. as enlargement approaches, many are getting cold feet, and anxiety is growing in both the current eu and the candidate countries. what we have all dreamed of and fought for for so many years - a united europe - is at the door and it is no wonder that questions are being raised. in the candidate countries where referendums are to be held, many citizens are probably wondering what they are getting into. within the eu, many questions arise about costs and how the eu should function. extremist parties exploit enlargement in their politics and play on people's fear of the unknown. political leadership is therefore needed more than ever. the leaders of the candidate countries must continue with determination the reforms required before the negotiations can be concluded. they've done an impressive job and there are still some questions. it's about going all the way and being honest. some domestic problems will still remain after membership there is life after the entry date. we in the eu also have a great responsibility: to tell the citizens about the enormous importance of enlargement and what is at stake. who thought it would be free? of course, expanding costs money, but in the long run, we're all winners. we must also draw up a contingency plan in the event of a 'no' vote in ireland. i think the european parliament has taken its responsibility. we have pushed the enlargement process hard, demanded a date and carefully reviewed progress. in the report we are adopting today, it is crystal clear that we support enlargement, while at the same time highlighting some problems. we too have a responsibility to convince the citizens of this historic task. this is a historic opportunity to unite the entire continent. together we can solve common problems: environmental degradation, crime, growth, security and the safeguarding of democracy and humanist values. there is little time left and i am convinced that the copenhagen meeting in december will be historic and will lead to the biggest and most important enlargement of the eu to date. the enlargement is irreversible and absolutely necessary. the alternative is chaos, uncertainty and a fateful division of europe.
madam president, i would like to thank you. i would like to highlight paragraph 26 of the karl-erik olsson report on agriculture and enlargement. it is about initiating talks with the candidate countries on the shape of a reform of the eu's agricultural policy. i have been working on the food safety package for almost three years and have been thinking about how much the reform will affect each of the candidate countries. it is important that they should be involved now, that they should be involved in shaping the european union and the policies with which they will actually live. i am sure they all realise that it is more important to them what the policy that is to replace agenda 2000 looks like than what cash contributions they receive in the year or two before that.

"the first f is the bailiff, the commander of the harsh; he is looking for your wealth, your inheritance and your farm" - so barked the swedish nobel prize winner erik axel karlfeldt at the turn of the last century. it is perhaps no surprise that taxes play a special role in my country, which has the highest tax rates in the world. however, it is not only in sweden that the issue of taxation, especially at eu level, is difficult to deal with. there is also a concern that the next step after a common exchange of information will be the harmonization of taxes, including direct taxes, as villiers and honeyball have just pointed out. we liberals are not willing to let the eu decide on direct taxes. however, we can of course see the benefits and the need for increased cooperation in this area as well. the aim is to increase the understanding of tax officials of the member states' tax systems that a country wants to reduce high taxes, for example to increase the level of education and thus improve the functioning of the tax system in the internal market, to combat fraud and to promote developments in the field of data, and to ensure full involvement of the candidate countries. we therefore support the commission proposal and the report by mr garcía margallo y marfil, we share the rapporteur's remarks on the vat system and the principle of the country of origin and we also support the amendments by the committee on budgetary control. allow me to stress the importance of compliance with the applicable eu and national rules on privacy and the use of personal data. this is important, not least in the tax field.

commission. – mr president, i would like to begin by thanking the european parliament and in particular the rapporteur, mr berenguer fuster, for their judicious and efficient handling of this matter. after the second reading in the committee on economic and monetary affairs, only 18 amendments were submitted to plenary. this is evidence of the balanced quality of the common position text which mr berenguer fuster mentioned to the committee last week. it was not just a compliment. indeed, the common position to a large extent follows the line taken by parliament at first reading. it took account of the most important amendments adopted, which improved our initial proposal. the action plan for financial services identified insurance intermediaries as a priority objective. insurance intermediaries are indeed confronted with obstacles which prevent them from taking full advantage of the freedoms of the treaty. this hinders the proper functioning of the internal market. the directive following the text of the common position will ensure that, once registered in his home member state, which is responsible for checking professional and financial requirements, an insurance intermediary will be able to conduct his activities throughout the internal market. that will improve the functioning of that internal market in insurance, particularly as regards retail markets. at the same time, the directive will guarantee the protection of customers by setting appropriate information requirements. the council common position now submitted to this parliament is fully consistent with the essential objectives pursued in the commission's proposal. the commission supports this text. but it is vital to ensure that the difficult balance reached in the common position is not put in jeopardy by amendments which would not be compatible with its fundamental objectives. let me now turn to the amendments which were submitted to plenary. the commission can accept the following amendments: amendments nos 1, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 10, 11, 12, 15 and 16. with regard to amendment no 17, which aims to clarify the scope of the directive, the commission can accept it, even though it considers that minor redrafting is needed in order to avoid any misunderstanding. the commission is not, however, in a position to accept the following amendments: amendments nos 2, 9, 13, 14 and 18, for the following reasons: firstly, amendment no 2, on which mr berenguer fuster, mr tannock and mr schmidt have spoken just now. that amendment aims to exclude from the scope of the proposal a large number of insurance intermediaries, such as car dealers selling motor insurance, travel agencies, veterinary surgeons, captive intermediaries of industrial firms and some bank insurance activities. such exclusions would affect the sale of a large number of insurance products involving important risks. they would deprive the directive of a major part of its content. furthermore these excluded intermediaries would not be able to make use of the advantages provided for in the directive. should these intermediaries be excluded from the benefits of the internal market, i very much share mr berenguer fuster's concern about that amendment. the commission cannot accept it. amendment no 9 introduces a transitional provision aiming to facilitate the application of the directive to those intermediaries already carrying out activities before the date of adoption of the proposal by the commission, namely september 2000. the commission already welcomed this idea at first reading, but, as amendment no 15 better meets the concerns of the directive, the commission would prefer it to be adopted rather than amendment no 9.while a fundamental element of the proposal and the common position is to ensure a high level of consumer protection by providing customers with appropriate information and advice, amendment no 13 would devalue that requirement. it provides that the advice given by an intermediary has to be based on the best possible advice principle. the commission cannot accept that amendment. it would imply enormous difficulties for the work of any insurance intermediary. it would also lead to great legal uncertainty and may be a source of legal conflict. last, but not least, it would increase the costs of insurance mediation. the text of the common position already contains a balanced provision which makes it mandatory to provide appropriate advice on the basis of a fair analysis of the insurance contracts available on the market. that provision fully ensures a high level of consumer protection. amendment no 16, which refers to an objective analysis, is along the same lines as the common position and may improve the text. the last amendment that i wish to discuss is amendment no 14, which provides, in the case of immediate cover, for a derogation to the principle that the information requested by the directive must be given in writing. that would jeopardise the objective of a high level of consumer protection. the common position already takes account of the spirit of amendment no 44 proposed at first reading. accepting this new amendment would deprive the consumer of written confirmation of the information required by the directive. the same reasoning also applies to amendment no 18, which reduces the obligation on insurance intermediaries to specify the demands of the customer.
- thank you, mr. commissioner frits bolkestein. - i'm not sure i can do that. the debate is closed. the vote will take place on thursday at 12 noon.
- the next item is the report (a5-0177/2002) by mrs catherine stihler, on behalf of the committee on fisheries, on the proposal for a council regulation establishing measures to restore the stocks of cod and hake (com (2002) 724 - c5-0095/2001 - 0299 (cns)).
commission. – madam president, may i begin by saying that i shall not speak about the proposal itself, other than to emphasise that the commission regards this as one of the most important steps to be undertaken under the common fisheries policy. as many here are aware, this proposal foreshadows many aspects to be discussed in future on the overall reform of the common fisheries policy. if we cannot collaborate to secure recovery of our fish stocks, the future of fish and fishermen cannot be ensured. let me now comment on the amendments. on amendment no 1, the commission cannot accept this amendment, which would remove reference to hake from the proposal. ices has advised that the stock of hake is in danger of collapse. if this is the case, the commission must act to rectify this situation. the only alternative assessment of the state of the stock is that prepared at the request of the instituto español de oceanografía by canadian fishery scientist, mr maguire. during the discussions in council working parties, spain has not challenged the validity of the ices assessment of the historical development of the hake stock or of the current state of that stock. spain has challenged one of the so-called precautionary reference points against which the state of the stock is judged. in detail, the spanish officials have proposed that the value of 165 000 tonnes in the commission proposal should be changed to 145 000 tonnes. this position does not contradict the perception that the stock is in danger of collapse. it perhaps means that it is in less danger, but the dangers still exist. regarding amendment no 2, the commission feels that this amendment is unnecessary since account is already consistently taken of scientific reports on environmental effects, to the extent that these effects are scientifically documented and quantified. an example is the effect of low salinity in recent years on reproductive success of cod in the baltic. the other factors mentioned in the amendment lie beyond the scope of the common fisheries policy and therefore it would be inappropriate to refer to them in this proposal. furthermore it remains a fact that for cod, hake and many other fish stocks, fishing is the main cause of mortality and the reduction of fishing is the most likely way to achieve recovery of the stocks. regarding amendment no 3, the commission is already working on improvement of all aspects of scientific advice. any communication on this topic will be included in the second package of reform proposals, which will be presented to this parliament in the near future. furthermore, it is not correct to say that the necessary analysis of the correlation between fishing effort and mortality rates or the appropriate level of tax has not been made for the year 2002. for those reasons the commission cannot accept that amendment. regarding amendment no 4, the commission can agree in principle with this analysis, since tacs are not instruments which will necessarily solve the problem of discards. the problem will only be solved if selectivity of fishing gears is increased or if fishing is reduced or prohibited in geographical areas in which small fish congregate. that aspect has been dealt with in a series of commission and council regulations emanating from the emergency situation with cod and hake. also the commission is currently working on an action plan on discards in the framework of the cfp reform. this amendment is therefore not necessary. regarding amendment no 5, the commission cannot accept this amendment, since it takes the view that public aid for vessel modernisation should be used only for the safety of fishing vessels, whereas the amendment implies that other objectives might also be eligible for aid. aid which is likely to enhance the fishing power of fleets is no longer acceptable. regarding amendment no 6, the commission cannot accept this amendment since real time closures need to be enacted very rapidly. the inclusion of the council in this mechanism would take much more time. once the council has taken a decision, real time closure would no longer be efficient. amendment no 7 is accepted: there has been a typographical error. regarding amendment no 8, the commission is in the process of considering the impact of a lower annual variation in tac level on stock recovery. that is a point on which the council has also expressed some reservations. regarding amendment no 9, the commission cannot accept that amendment. technical measures are dealt with in a different legislative proposal. technical measures which are acceptable to member states will bring about some improvement to the present situation, but do not go far enough. the commission indicated earlier that only about 20% of the desired reduction in fishing mortality will be achieved by the application. a limitation of fishing effort is therefore also required. compulsory scrapping of vessels is not mentioned in the proposal and it is not intended for the future cfp. tacs will be proposed at levels concordant with scientific advice. regarding amendment no 10, the commission cannot accept that amendment. hake are not caught only by industrial fishing vessels and cod are caught in very small quantities. a proposal for 5% reflects the probable catches of cod and is therefore proportionate to the objective pursued. the commission cannot accept amendment no 11. while it is difficult to calculate the amount of a supplementing premium needed to generate more interest in scrapping, 20% is already a significant increase and corresponds to previous practice in similar situations, for example structural measures for the ex-morocco fleet in spain and portugal. the commission cannot accept amendment no 12. we see no reason to restrict safety-related aid to smaller vessels, that is, those of less than 12 metres. the recent proposals for cfp reform propose that all vessels should be eligible for such aid, provided that it does not affect the fishing capacity of the vessel. the commission cannot accept amendment no 13. this is a very expensive form of aid, which does not reduce the underlying problem of over-capacity. only short-term measures of this kind can be justified. to accept community co-financing for a longer period would significantly reduce the availability of community funds for co-financing of scrapping, which is clearly the priority objective in this case. amendment no 14 is not acceptable to the commission, since the community cannot accept full responsibility for aid of this kind. co-financing is a fundamental principle for structural aid. the commission cannot accept amendment no 15. a specification of the duration of the plan is not acceptable. the stocks will be deemed to have recovered whenever, for two consecutive years, science indicates that quantities of mature fish have been in excess of defined levels. reference is made in the last paragraph of the explanatory memorandum to the commission's intention to conduct an impact study within 18 months of adoption of the proposal. this might be supported by the addition of texts in an article of the proposal, but the reference in the explanatory memorandum should be sufficient. regarding amendment no 16, a review process can be envisaged, but not on an annual basis. it will take some time for the proposed measures to take effect. the earliest point in time would be after two full years of operation. reference is made in the last paragraph of the explanatory memorandum to the commission's intention to conduct an impact study within 18 months of adoption of the proposal. this might be supported by the addition of texts in an article of the proposal, but the reference in the explanatory memorandum should be sufficient. i should like to refer to some additional remarks made by members of parliament. to begin with, mr varela suanzes-carpegna referred to the scientific advice on which we base our proposal. that advice is the advice of ices. despite its shortcomings, which nobody denies, the ices advice is by far the best available. the reform of the common fisheries policy prioritises the improvement of scientific advice. in the meantime we have to work with the best advice we have, which is the ices advice. this is also in reply to mrs miguélez ramos.
- thank you, mr. commissioner frits bolkestein. - i'm not sure i can do that. the debate is closed. the vote will take place tomorrow at 12 noon.
- the next item is the report (a5-0198/2002) by mrs maría del pilar ayuso gonzález, on behalf of the committee on agriculture and rural development, on the common organization of the market in ethyl alcohol of agricultural origin (com (2001) 101 - c5-0095/2001 - 2001/0055 (cns)).
commission. – mr president, may i begin by thanking the members of this parliament for the work they have put into considering the proposal for a common market organisation for alcohol. in particular my thanks go to the rapporteur, mrs ayuso gonzález, for her analysis of the proposal and the report. before i comment in more detail on the amendments proposed, i would remind you that this is another proposal to establish a set of common rules for the alcohol sector after several attempts in the past, all of which failed for various reasons. the commission is of the opinion that the actual market situation in this sector makes it necessary to propose at least a minimal set of rules and that, accordingly, the chances of success this time are fairly good. most of the amendments proposed can be accepted by the commission. all amendments relating to the change of title are acceptable. indeed if the title proposed by the commission creates problems because it refers more to an intervention system not provided for here, a more appropriate title reflecting the content of the regulation as suggested by parliament can be considered. the amendments on including synthetic alcohol in the article on market information can also be accepted. parliament initially proposed to extend the proposal to synthetic alcohol, which is not an annex i product and therefore is not part of the common agricultural policy. for most outlets except alcoholic beverages, both agricultural and synthetic alcohol can be used and therefore compete on the same market. the suggestion in the agriculture committee's report that synthetic alcohol should be included in the article on data collection to establish a yearly market balance of the sector, and to have better market information and increased market transparency, is one which the commission is willing to consider. amendments 7 and 19 concerning deletion of the state aids article are not acceptable. the commission is of the opinion that all provisions proposed are necessary to establish a set of common rules for the alcohol sector, including the provisions on application of the rules on state aids. without fair application of those rules, there would not be a real common set of rules for agricultural alcohol, and i would point out that those rules already apply to synthetic alcohol. the commission is well aware of the concern that the application of state aid rules may create a problem for the functioning of the german alcohol monopoly. i would repeat that the purpose of this proposal on agricultural alcohol is not to put an end to that monopoly. if alcohol production by german farmers under monopoly rules, including federal payments, is necessary to maintain valuable eco-systems or to cover the extra costs of environment-friendly production methods, current state aid rules offer substantial scope to grant aids for those purposes or rural development aid schemes could be used. on the other hand, small distillers producing not only alcohol but also spirits could benefit from the de minimis rule on state aid for non-agricultural products. the commission is willing to try and find a solution, but cannot accept the proposal to simply delete the related article on state aid from the draft text. a solution should be found within the context of that article. amendment no 8, which refers to the declaration on rum under the acp-eu partnership agreement and asks for special treatment to rum producers affected by the application of eu policies is also difficult to accept. by definition, the proposed regulation should comply with all bilateral and international agreements, as stated in article 14. it is neither necessary nor appropriate to grant special treatment to one category of partners within a specific agreement. amendment no 13 can be accepted except for the reference to cn code 3814 0090 in the definition of non-agricultural alcohol because that code covers mixtures of alcohol with solvents and is not limited to non-agricultural alcohol. adoption of regulation no 142/2002 has solved the problem of imports of those mixtures. amendments nos 14, 16, 17 and 18 are purely technical improvements to the text that have also been discussed in the council, and there is no problem in accepting them. amendment no 20, however, is more difficult to accept. the commission does not want to create a specific working group on alcohol as part of the wine management committee. the commission's aim is the same as that in the amendment, that is, to bring together the real experts on alcohol. for that reason, the commission has already made a statement on organising the work of the wine management committee in such a way that all alcohol-related items are grouped together in order to permit member states to send the real experts to the meeting. although amendment no 21 is acceptable, i must point out that it limits the commission's scope to take transitional measures more than the original proposal does. this text appears in other regulations and is therefore acceptable. as far as amendments nos 22 and 24 are concerned, the commission of course agrees that the rules on state aid should be applied in this sector, too, and therefore should not be deleted. i also recognise the value of existing aid systems in certain member states, especially for small agricultural distilleries, and the need to find a solution for them. the notion of a transitional measure is certainly one i must give thought to, but i am not yet in a position to indicate the specific nature of the solution. the commission cannot accept amendment no 23, as indicated a short time ago. the commission can accept amendments nos 3, 5, 13 and 15 on including synthetic alcohol in the proposal as far as the system of data collection is concerned. according to the court of justice case law, it can be included without changing the legal basis of the proposal and therefore the commission is of the opinion that the global alcohol market is satisfactorily addressed by this proposal. i hope that my comments today have clarified the commission's position on this proposal. moreover, i should like to thank members of parliament for their constructive suggestions, which we shall of course consider, and for the time they have devoted to this process, and in particular i would thank the rapporteur, mrs ayuso gonzález, for her contribution.
- thank you, mr. commissioner frits bolkestein. - i'm not sure i can do that. the debate is closed. the next item is the report (doc. the vote will take place tomorrow at 12 noon. (the sitting was closed at 11.45 p.m.)
the rapporteur. mr president, i would like to thank you. every year, 42,000 people die on the roads of the eu. this is a very big political issue for people in the eu. in the white paper on transport, the commission says that we must halve the number of deaths by 2010. then we must be active, not passive. we must take all kinds of measures at all levels. everyone must help, not least industry, because we know that consumers demand this. security is a selling point. how, then, should cars be designed to be as gentle as possible to unprotected road users? the proposal for a voluntary agreement which we have received from the commission and which the car industry has negotiated is inadequate. it is astonishing that the commission has been able to accept it in its present form. there are uncertainties in implementation and the eevc tests are not taken seriously, despite having been developed for more than 22 years. this is about life on the road and life in our community, and we have to be vigilant. the case has been difficult to deal with and i would like to thank all the shadow rapporteurs in the committee for a very successful work, which has required a lot of us all. there have been many different opinions on this matter, many lobbyists and many views from the committees. it is now important to start from the point of view of road safety and consumer requirements. there are three options in our response to the commission. the first is that we accept the voluntary agreement. secondly, we are in favour of a detailed directive which specifies exactly how cars are to be designed. thirdly, we are in favour of a framework directive. the committee does not consider the voluntary agreement to be sufficient. it is too uncertain and does not allow for any popular parliamentary scrutiny of the way in which the car industry takes its responsibilities. it is less binding and not sufficiently transparent in the process. the committee therefore proposes a framework directive, which the commission also mentions in its communication. this means that we set the targets in the political groups, and then experts monitor implementation under the supervision and with the involvement of the commission. the framework directive which we are now proposing and calling for contains several components. the objective is that all 2010 car models will meet the requirements of the four eevc texts. this process must begin now. the eevc is the most advanced institution in road safety research worldwide, particularly in the field of pedestrian safety. however, other test methods can be envisaged which provide at least the same level of protection for pedestrians. of course, there must be effective tests that affect the design of cars. we also want the process to be evaluated regularly and that annual reports be sent to the european parliament. this way we become part of the process and can check that the objectives we have set are actually being met. we want the member states, through their type-approval authorities, to be involved in the evaluation of the implementation of the framework directive. we also believe that the eevc is at the cutting edge of testing methods and can be further developed. these are the demands of the committee. we expect the commission and commissioner liikanen to confirm that he will be presenting a proposal for a framework directive. in the socialist group we would have liked to go further in certain sections, for example to emphasise the exclusive merits of the eevc tests much more strongly and to set a clear sub-goal to get a straight line in the development of pedestrian protection from now until 2010. the socialist group also wants the commission to present its framework directive immediately, preferably before the end of the year. this is important if the commission is to show that it is serious about the matter. as i said, this is a very controversial matter. we have now reached a compromise and will therefore support it in order to get as much support as possible for the call for a framework directive as the best solution to the road safety issue. that is why my group will support the committee's proposals in the report and i would urge other members to do the same. we accept the compromise which, for the sake of the road users, i am glad we were able to negotiate.
mr president, i would like to thank you. in paragraph 48 the socialists want to add the name 'the former yugoslav republic of macedonia'. in the original version, it is simply called "macedonia". this is not a linguistic error, of course, but a highly political issue. the liberals are suggesting that we vote on the original version.
mr president, i would like to thank you. i think there was a mistake in the translation. the original text reads 'macedonia', but the socialists have submitted a comment on adding 'the former yugoslav republic of macedonia'. this is not a linguistic change, but a political one. we want the vote to be on the text as it stands, that is, only 'macedonia'.
mr president, i would like to thank you. there is an error in the swedish text. it says 'increased access for mediterranean countries', but it should be 'developing countries'. i hope the other language versions are correct.
the rapporteur. mr president, i would like to thank you. there is an error in amendment no 1 in the swedish text. the correct version is the increase in the total eu quota is kept within the limits proposed by the commission, but the swedish text says the decrease.
in writing. -sv) we voted against this report in the final vote, although it contains some points which improve the commission's proposal, such as the new chapter on important socioeconomic impact assessments and the somewhat larger reduction in fishing effort proposed for hake and cod. however, we believe that, particularly in view of the urgently needed programme of reform of the common fisheries policy recently presented by the commission, this report in many respects unfortunately prejudges the overall approach required for this reform. in this context, we consider it particularly unfortunate that the report proposes that the eu should pay compensation in full to fishermen and ship owners affected by the temporary cessation of their activities. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's structural funds are used to finance the necessary measures. we believe that the commission's proposal, which provides for the co-financing of these compensations, should be preferred.
in writing. - we are in favour of eu enlargement in line with the will of the people of the candidate countries to join, and we voted in favour of the report. however, we cannot support certain passages in it. firstly, the general support for the rapprochement of the candidate countries with nato and the reference to nato as an important element of european security and stability. we believe that nato's role is much more complex than that. we would therefore have thought it better not to include this debate in this report. secondly, we voted against all the amendments to article 50 because the bens decrees are an implementation of the decisions taken by the allies at potsdam. these decisions led to the exclusion of all germans who had always preferred to live in germany and thus wanted to oppress the non-german population. this exclusion is worth supporting, even if and precisely because it still has legal force.
in writing. - i voted against the report. the areas of the union sharing common borders with the candidate countries will feel the effects of enlargement more quickly and more directly than the other member states. the christian democrats therefore have no objection to the commission's proposal to allocate them special funds to improve their infrastructure and to promote, for example, youth exchanges with the border regions of the applicant countries. we do not, however, think that there is any need to provide additional funds in relation to the commission's proposal. we believe that it is unjustified to give small and mediumsized enterprises additional aid long in advance in order to enable them to compete with the applicant countries. on the contrary, they must see enlargement as a positive challenge. the christian democrats are against the idea of applying, as the report calls for, transitional periods limited in time to freedom of services and to the free movement of employees, on the basis of an agreement between the trade unions and local businesses. in practice, this would amount to creating a second-class membership of the eu. finally, we question the need to give these 23 regions of finland, germany, austria, italy and greece more aid than the border regions between the applicant countries and their eastern neighbours.
in writing. - (sv) we abstained in the final vote on the belder report on access to public internet sites. the report highlights a number of problems faced by the disabled and the elderly, which are important and deserve our attention. creating the conditions for disabled people to participate fully in society is one of the very first priorities on the liberal agenda. however, we believe that it is up to the member states to take responsibility for measures to adapt these public internet sites. in general, we must also adopt a very restrictive attitude towards the regulation of internet services, because the dynamics of the web is based precisely on the fact that it develops without regulation or subsidies. we believe in the principle of subsidiarity, we believe that decisions should be taken as closely as possible to the people they affect. that is why we insist so much on the question of the drafting of a european constitution, in which the distribution of responsibilities would be clear to all. it must be clear to all citizens that the eu should only deal with the issues it is best placed to deal with - that is, those of a cross-border nature. all other issues must be dealt with at local, regional or national level.
mr president, i would like to thank you. i fully agree with schroedter and wiersma and will summarize in a few points how we christian democrats view the closure of the osce office in minsk. first, the closure is not an act of force on the part of belarus. rather, the incident is an expression of weakness. secondly, belarus is actually signalling that it is seeking contact with the eu, albeit in a desperate way. thirdly, belarus is seeking a negotiating card, something to play against the european union. fourthly, belarus is trying to make the basic need to address border issues and the refugee situation in belarus with the european union more acute. on the basis of this analysis, we can enter into discussions with belarus with confidence, in which we will of course take as a starting point our strong and fundamental criticism of the unacceptable events in minsk at the osce office. i appeal to the commission and to the liikanen, which i urge to address personally to commissioner patten, to intensify all contacts with belarus. the events in minsk with the osce office are a sign that belarus is making a desperate plea for some form of contact. they are doing so in an unacceptable way, but we cannot accept that neighbours of an enlarged european union behave in the way that has happened in this case. it is also important that the european parliament clearly states that president putin in russia, within the framework of the union between belarus and russia, has a great and special responsibility to exert pressure on belarus to act in an internationally credible manner.
mr president, i would like to thank you. the abolition of the death penalty is a cornerstone of the moral message that the eu wants to convey globally, not only to democratic and free countries, but also, and not least, to dictatorships. the abolition of the death penalty also expresses the human view of the equal, unique and inviolable dignity of the human person, which is deeply rooted in the judeo-christian ethics of our western civilization. the eu's position on the death penalty is therefore based on a conscious and deeply rooted ethical and moral foundation on which the human vision of our civilization rests. the enlargement of the union also extends the principle of abolition of the death penalty to new countries in eastern europe and the former soviet union. we christian democrats and liberal conservatives in the ppe-de group regret that the assertion of this unifying principle of abolition of the death penalty, which we fully support, in the resolution in question is only directed at free and democratic states such as japan and south korea, which are currently hosting the world cup, and taiwan. for some reason the socialist group, together with the liberals, the greens and the left socialist and former communist parties here in the european parliament, have chosen not to say anything at all about the abolition of the death penalty in neighbouring countries such as china and north korea. we find this very difficult to understand and one-sided and therefore choose to abstain in the vote at 5.30 p.m. in the fight to abolish the death penalty, we must stand on a united moral foundation. the exclusive focus on free and democratic states gives a one-sided impression. this is deeply regrettable.

the commission is aware of the fact that the commission has not yet taken a decision on the matter, and that it is not yet in a position to take a decision on the matter. we all know that the health of the road transport sector is fundamental to the flow of intra-community trade and the european economy, and that we are talking about one of the essential freedoms of the basic pillars of the internal market and the constitution of the communities. at the same time, we know that the sector is going through a difficult period: the pressure of competition - which is very strong - the problems of congestion in many corridors, the difficulties in transit in some places - such as the alps, for example - the concerns about road safety or the strong social pressure. these problems do not merely distort the image of the sector, but are real problems, which may be aggravated by enlargement. the commission therefore stressed the urgent need to adopt a set of measures for a community strategy to promote coherence in the road transport sector and to strengthen the single internal market in this sector. the commission has already presented a number of measures, many of them during the french presidency, which were also partly discussed and which form part of our analysis of the white paper. in this context we have to situate the proposal on the traffic restrictions we are discussing today. i strongly believe that it is important to introduce community-wide rules in this area and to organise restrictions on heavy goods transport on weekends throughout the union. seven of the 15 states already impose restrictions on certain categories of vehicles. the lack of coordination of such measures makes it difficult for the internal market in road transport to function, and the commission and a majority of member states are in favour of common rules establishing a durable, clear and transparent system which facilitates, as has been said, a combination of a difficult balance between two opposing interests: the right of citizens to have roads that are not absolutely congested at weekends and the freedom of transit of goods and merchandise through the union. the current amended commission proposal has sought to address the initial objections by clearly stating that it applies only to heavy goods vehicles on international routes and on the european transport network. it therefore remains the responsibility of the member states to impose restrictions of varying duration on all roads outside this network, including other national roads. the proposal harmonises the timetable for weekend restrictions, but does not make existing restrictions in the vast majority of eu countries any more difficult. it also provides for a system for notifying the days, including public holidays, on which member states intend to impose restrictions. i therefore answer mr jarzembowski's question and thank him for his support. in principle, we share mr savary's request and there are only a few questions to be answered as regards the application of a cost-benefit analysis and the intensity of the information mechanism envisaged there. as regards the specific amendments, i would say that most of them can be accepted, 20 in particular. but there are some which we must reject, in particular amendment no 32, simply because it rejects our proposal. we also reject amendments nos 4, 13, 35 and 38, because they allow the member states to introduce a restriction and simply inform the commission and, by extension, other member states and their carriers. we think that this is an excessive inflexibility which cannot be allowed; that the comitology system is the right one, which maintains and guarantees everyone. nor can we support amendments nos 24 to 28, 30, 36, 37 and 40, which introduce an overly restrictive approach, which would unbalance the proposal and reduce its quality. amendments nos 18, 29 and 31 would add additional requirements which the commission cannot assume because they are disproportionate to the proposed objective; i am referring to burdens such as the compilation of statistics, lists of infringements and fines, and further study of road safety aspects, not to mention the internal market. finally, we reject amendments nos 2, 23, 25 and part of no 39, because we do not think they are relevant. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to reiterate once again my thanks to mr savary for his tireless work. we must have the courage to face this report and not be discouraged, because i understand that the positions are so opposed that an agreement seems impossible. but i think, like mr savary - and that is why i have made this amended proposal and have continued to insist on it - that we must find a middle ground which will obviously not satisfy anyone, but which can be a balance which takes account in a reasonable way of the various interests on the table. the fact is that we must also remember that the internal market will soon be extended to the east and that, in that situation, it will be even more necessary to seek a coordinated position and approach, a certain degree of harmonization. it is not a question of total harmonization, but of a minimum of harmonization in the rules on restrictions on movement, in the transeuropean networks, which will allow us to guarantee adequate freedom of movement of goods within the union, which is absolutely essential for the economic development and economic activity of our countries. thank you very much, madam president, thank you very much, mr. savary. i hope that tomorrow this parliament will finally be able to adopt the savary report, which i consider to be balanced and the best we can achieve on both sides.

- the following two reports are requested for joint debate: - a5-0218/2002 by mr mayol i raynal, on behalf of the committee on economic and monetary affairs and industrial policy, on the possibility of applying a reduced rate of excise duty on certain mineral oils containing biofuels and on biofuels (com (2001) 547 - c5-0030/2002 - 2001/0266 (cns)); - a5-0244/2002 by mrs ayuso gonzález, on behalf of the committee on industry, foreign trade, research and energy, on the promotion of the use of biofuels in transport (com (2001) 547 - c5-0684/2001 - 2001/0265 (cod))
madam president, i would like to thank you. as the european union is committed to reducing greenhouse gas emissions, i believe it is extremely important to provide good conditions for the development of renewable energy, especially biofuels. however, reducing emissions is not the only reason why it is important to invest in development work to develop alternative fuels. the question of the long-term security of energy supply for the european union is also becoming increasingly important. since taxes represent a very large part of the selling price of energy products, various types of tax incentives are effective instruments of control. the proposal i am going to limit myself to discussing concerns the possibility of reducing excise duties in the member states, i.e. the commission's proposal to amend directive 92/81/eec. the proposal provides member states with a flexible financial instrument to develop the production of biofuels. it takes account of the budgetary situation of the member states, local conditions and their own choice of technical solutions. the proposal is therefore also in line with the principle of subsidiarity. the amendments tabled by the rapporteur and the committee on economic and monetary affairs and industrial policy and adopted in committee have, in my view, further improved the proposal. they include requirements for time limits and monitoring, as well as requirements for the evaluation of overall energy efficiency and environmental impact assessment and study. i am therefore happy to support the report and i hope that it will be adopted by the european parliament.
madam president, i would like to thank you. using biofuels can be very good for the environment, not least for greenhouse gas emissions. however, this is not necessarily the case, but depends entirely on how these biofuels are produced. using heavily subsidised, highly processed agricultural products to produce biofuels can in many cases be unwise from an environmental point of view. there are much more interesting technologies, such as the production of ethanol from forest raw materials, in terms of the overall environmental impact. biofuels must therefore be assessed in the context of their overall environmental impact if a long-term sustainable system is to be achieved. taxation must also be adapted to this long-term environmental impact. i also believe that if we are to achieve a breakthrough in this new technology and the large-scale investment required, countries must be allowed to completely eliminate the tax during a transitional period, in order to make the necessary investments in engines, petrol stations and so on.
mr president, i would like to thank you. in all activities, safety is of central importance, but in nuclear activities, safety is crucial. there must be no room for human error, no possibility of sabotage or of exposing installations or nuclear material to acts of terrorism. it is therefore gratifying that the euratom report shows a high level of safety and increased awareness of the risks. the committee unanimously agreed to focus further on safety and measures to strengthen the safety concerns of all those involved in nuclear work. i would like to draw particular attention to the training of staff, the increase in transport safety and the increased risk of terrorist acts with which we all live after 11 september 2001. mr president, i would like to thank you. nuclear power is a highly debated and controversial method of energy production. we've had a number of serious accidents and quite a few incidents that could have ended very badly if not for the safety systems and personnel that were on top of their game. safety issues are therefore at the very heart of all nuclear management and must never be an exception. public concern about nuclear power, nuclear material and the issues associated with these activities must always be taken very seriously.
the rapporteur. mr president, commissioner, members and the audience! the european parliament is trying to ensure that the 2003 budget is a genuine reform budget. there are two perspectives at the heart of this. the first is, of course, the historic enlargement, and the second is the need to reform the european union in various areas. the bulk of the budgetary work will of course take place in the autumn, but we hope to be able to resolve some important issues at the july conciliation at the council's first reading. the document we are debating and voting on today is about giving the european parliament's delegation a special mandate for this reconciliation with the danish presidency and the commission. the interinstitutional agreement governing these conciliations identifies three areas which it will deal with in particular. this is the case for agriculture, fisheries agreements and the common foreign and security policy. i suspect that we will not get very far this year in terms of agricultural and fisheries policy. as regards agriculture, we are awaiting the commission's proposal for a mid-term review, and many concrete details are still missing as regards the reform of the fisheries policy. we will of course raise these issues. there are some specific demands on agriculture which we wish to address. the reform of the agricultural policy should involve the transfer of resources from the traditional market systems to rural development. we will also put forward a number of demands relating to the environmental aspect, the prevention of new disasters involving animal diseases, etc. we hope that some of the points will be taken up in july. as far as fisheries is concerned, we are asking in particular for information on the council's plans for the future, and we hope to be able to secure the funds needed to conclude further fisheries agreements with a number of important nations. we hope, of course, that this will be possible without the drama of last year, when the whole agreement with morocco broke down and thus created a difficult situation for all budgetary work. we are still living with the fact that we must also fulfil the promise we made last year of an additional 27 million to support the conversion of fishermen in spain and portugal. i think we will be able to resolve some of the details of these questions, but we have also dealt with some other main points. let me start with foreign and security policy. we hope that we can have a serious discussion on the funds needed, especially so that the eu can deploy a new police force in bosnia to replace the military troops we have had there. we also hope that we can have a first discussion on some other important points. we would at least need a little more information on what the commission and the council intend to do with regard to the operations in afghanistan and the middle east. this is essential if we are to solve the budgetary problems of external expenditure. our assessment is that there are too few resources. it is a structural problem that we have too little resources in category 4, i.e. expenditure on foreign policy and aid. we need some kind of new resource supply. this could be done in accordance with the proposals put forward by the commission, but which we know the council does not like, concerning a specific flexibility instrument for external expenditure. the european parliament has also put forward another alternative in this context, namely a more active use of the emergency aid reserve. it would be very good if we could get a solution to this in july. discussions on enlargement are likely to be the dominant issue in july. we must discuss, above all, the administrative resources needed to prepare for enlargement in the eu institutions. i want to emphasize that we are careful about this. the timetable for enlargement must be respected. preparations must therefore begin as soon as possible. it is very important to reach an agreement on category 5 administrative expenditure in the july conciliation. however, our prioritizing enlargement does not mean that we can automatically add more money to our staff and other administrative expenditure. we must also be able to demonstrate that we can implement active, new priorities, particularly in the administration. within the eu, as in any modern administration, we must show that we can develop our ways of working, that we can re-prioritize and that we can become more efficient and modern before we ask for more money. the committee on budgets has therefore decided to reject the use of the flexibility instrument in category 5. we're saying it's against the rules. the starting point should not be the use of flexibility instruments. we now know that the commission is requesting funds for 500 new posts to deal with enlargement. i am convinced that these services are needed, but we must still ask a number of questions before we simply agree to such an increase. today, over 800 people in the commission are working on enlargement, including some 300 in dg enlargement. once the great enlargement cycle is complete, it must be possible to reallocate these resources to work with the enlarged union. we need further background before we can simply agree to an automatic increase of 500 posts. the same applies to the relex reform. the reform of the administration of foreign and aid policy promised in 2000 that further reform would be carried out in 2002 and a review. the commission's annual policy statement has promised a further review of the relex directorate's cooperation and efficiency. we want to see results on these points before we say yes to 500 new jobs. we're also launching two new projects. this concerns a project on an extended crossborder project with sme cooperation throughout the union and a project on simplification of the administration throughout the union. it is an extremely urgent project. (applause) i would like to thank you.
mr president, i would like to thank you. when we laid down the guidelines for the 2003 budget a few months ago, there was a very broad consensus in the european parliament on three priorities for category five. it was about preparing for the coming enlargement, it was about the necessary institutional reforms, and it was about managing both enlargement and institutional reforms within the budgetary framework. if the european council in copenhagen in december decides to make a big bang, with ten new countries joining in time for the next european elections in 2004, this will be extremely demanding. no challenge is greater than this one! the european parliament has pursued a forward-looking policy for a long time and has worked with determination to make the forthcoming enlargement possible. it is a work which is now continuing into the 2003 budget. if all this is to be achieved, enlargement must be the top priority of all the eu institutions and also have a visible impact on the ongoing budgetary work. it is in this context that it is positive and gratifying that the incoming danish presidency has made it very clear that enlargement is its first priority. this work must also be reflected in the eu's own organisation. this year, and not least from the european parliament, there has been a very clear indication that each institution must complete the preparations for enlargement by 2004. it makes special demands on next year's budget. the conciliation with the council in just over two weeks' time will be a very important part of this work, not least as regards the possibilities of reaching an agreement on category five.
