in writing. - (sv) the european union is based on the four freedoms. when we held the swedish referendum on membership, the yes side won because of the optimism that the citizens felt about the four freedoms. as part of the current enlargement, the new candidate countries will be subject to a period of quarantine in relation to the free movement of persons seeking employment in the various member states. by allowing existing member states to decide on access to this freedom, one of the most fundamental rights of the union has been decentralised to the benefit of the member states. the generosity will fluctuate. a nationalisation of existing fundamental rights in the union is contrary to the spirit of the treaty of rome and should therefore not take place. no one wants to put in place protection at the internal borders of the union. fanciful decisions like this risk jeopardizing basic freedoms.
in writing. the brok report contains numerous directives to the future member states, which at some points raise the question of whether the eu should intervene in these matters. the report also supports nato's plans to accept new countries as members. it is a policy with which we strongly disagree. the report also contains some very positive elements. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'competent authority' to cover the whole of the eu. of course, turkey will have to meet the requirements of human rights and democracy before any accession negotiations can be discussed, but we are against the reactionary thinking that wants to turn the eu into a christian union of the white it is tempting to use the word aryan race. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos is a very good one.
in writing. (sv) we, the swedish social democrats, would like to explain why we voted against amendment no 17 on article 2 (b) by giving this explanation of vote. the reason is that the definition of advertising cannot be applied under swedish law. the definition of advertising as a commercial communication is incompatible with the swedish constitution. fundamental law for freedom of expression. we would prefer to use the definition 'commercial advertising' which would allow us to exclude tobacco from freedom of expression without changing the constitution. as the term commercial communication is a broader concept that includes the promotion of the product, it is contrary to the swedish constitution. the term commercial advertising does not include newspaper articles, advertising of opinions, promotion of a product, artistic expression and any other form of commercial promotion which is not part of an advertisement. it is therefore not possible under swedish law to prohibit the advertising of tobacco products as a 'commercial announcement' or 'commercial statement' without first amending the country's constitution. we also believe that the public health perspective should be given a clearer and stronger role in the internal market. when we are asked to weigh competition against public health, we think that health should be given priority over economic interests.
in writing. - (sv) i voted in favour of the report in the final vote, but against the 25 amendments tabled by the committee on legal affairs and the internal market. as a swedish christian democrat, i had to support the european parliament's restrictive and winning line (311 votes in favour, 202 against and 39 abstentions). the directive prohibits advertising for tobacco products in newspapers, on radio and on the internet and makes sponsorship illegal. member states will retain the right to adopt such measures as they consider necessary to protect men's health in relation to the types of advertising not covered by this directive. i think it is very good that all the european medical organizations have unanimously supported this position.
in writing. - (sv) the proposal submitted by denmark does not offer sufficient guarantees as regards legal certainty. it is unacceptable that member states can confiscate property belonging to the accused or to a member of his family, who has nothing to do with the offence committed, if the convicted person cannot prove that he acquired the property lawfully. this brings us to something as revolutionary as the inversion of the burden of proof. the commission's amendments are satisfactory in that they attempt to reject the inversion of the burden of proof, but they are not explicit enough. a satisfactory piece of legislation must clearly state that only property arising from a proven offence, or of equivalent value, may be confiscated. we, the swedish christian democrats, believe that the drafting of the framework decision should be the subject of further discussion, so as not to end up with an unconsidered restriction of legal certainty. although i believe that the harmonization of eu legislation plays an important role in facilitating the fight against crime, i was forced, from the point of view of legal certainty, to abstain from voting on this issue.
in writing. (sv) we, the swedish social democrats, have decided to abstain from the final vote on this report. we have reached this decision because we feel that, while it stresses the importance of sustainable development in fisheries, the inclusion of environmental issues in the reform of the common fisheries policy and the further need to clarify the impact of industrial fishing on the environment, the report also contains many messages and statements that we cannot support. for example, we are against state subsidies for the fishing fleet, which is already too large in relation to the fish stocks, despite the fact that safety on some fishing vessels is rather poor. these vessels are better off being retired permanently rather than being upgraded to continue contributing to overfishing. the long-term objective of the fishing industry must be sustainable development. the fishing capacity must therefore be reduced to bring it into balance with existing fish stocks. that is beyond dispute. sustainable fishing is not the enemy of fishermen, it is their best friend. we also have many reservations about the view expressed in the report that no impact analysis to date has been able to demonstrate that further capacity reduction will benefit the environment, especially as we currently know very little about how the different parts of ecosystems interact. while it is true that the greater investment in the development of more and more research in the marine environment in the future will allow us to draw clearer conclusions, the precautionary principle dictates in any case that capacity reduction is a necessary if not sufficient measure.

madam president, i would like to thank you. pakistan, the seventh largest country in the world with a population of 140 million, has been ruled by a military regime under general musharraf since 12 october 1999. of course, the eyes of the world have been on pakistan since the general promised to hold elections within three years of the military coup to restore democracy and civilian rule to the country. a small, small part of this promise was kept: elections were held. but the mere holding of elections is no guarantee of democracy. equally important are the circumstances surrounding the election. firstly, the pre-election process in the form of legislative amendments to increase the power of the military regime and the ability to dissolve parliament and dismiss the government, regulatory amendments limiting the eligibility of 4 per cent of the population, restrictions on the ability to campaign. secondly, during the elections, in the form of pressure on individual politicians, different rules for different candidates from different parties, irregularities in the electoral process and restrictions on people's ability to vote. thirdly, after the elections, in the form of a clear reluctance to hand over power to the elected. all in all, this leads us to regret that we have to say today that we have lost an opportunity to restore democracy in pakistan. the consequences for the people of pakistan cannot be overstated. unemployment, poverty and illiteracy are on the rise. the only way to improve the living conditions of the people of pakistan is through democracy, the transfer of military power to civilian power and the possibility for a civilian and democratically elected government to implement reforms in education and employment. i consider it our duty and obligation here in the european parliament to give a clear and truthful account of the circumstances surrounding the elections, which we were also able to observe through a delegation from here who were present. we must also stress the importance of continuing the work of restoring democracy in pakistan. i therefore support cushnahan's statements here today. the government of pakistan must be urged to work in a democratic direction and the eu must continue to follow developments in pakistan closely and critically.

mr president, i would like to thank you. staffing companies are an essential part of the future flexible labour market. the recruitment problems of companies are increasingly solved by the recruitment of staff from staffing companies. immigrants often have good opportunities to gain a foothold in the labour market through these companies. it is therefore important to encourage and facilitate the activities of the staffing companies. we have heard here that the legislation governing the employment services varies greatly between the member states. it is therefore difficult to create even a framework legislation which takes account of all the differences. the political starting point of the proposal for a directive is to see work in staffing companies as a form of fixed-term employment and to prevent so-called conditional dumping. the problems of framework legislation at european level are made clear by looking at sweden. there the political principles have no counterpart in the actual situation. in sweden, the principle is to employ a person on a temporary basis. about 70% of the employees in the staffing companies are permanent employees. the conditions of employment of both permanent and temporary employees are regulated by collective agreements. individual and differentiated remuneration is applied to officials, which means that no salary comparisons are made. for workers, there are collective agreements that guarantee that wages are not lower than those in the client company and that the client company's working hours are respected. however, this is not regulated by law but by negotiation between the social partners, which should continue to be the case. that is why we do not think that wages should be included in the definition of working conditions in the directive. the directive also does not regulate any comparisons of pay and working conditions. the crew industry is doing well in sweden and could be a model for europe. look at that, jan andersson! the present proposal does not, however, facilitate the development of this sector in europe. europe does not need fewer forms of employment, but more.
mr president, i would like to thank you. the future offers both threats and opportunities. biotechnology and life sciences are fields that inspire both awe and delight. there is reason not to rush into thinking that this offers the solution to all the problems we see before us. at the same time, we should not be intimidated by the fact that we do not have all the answers. the union has identified life sciences as an area which should help make europe the most competitive knowledge-based economy. properly used, biotechnology can offer solutions to many of our common future problems. to meet the environmental threat, we need not only restrictions on our existence but also new findings that can help us to deal with the environmental threat in a technical, mechanical and biological way. in this context, biotechnology offers many opportunities. the fight against poverty and hunger in developing countries requires different approaches to succeed. we should not overestimate biotechnology, but we should not underestimate it either. crops that can withstand varying temperatures, drought, and moisture can help provide food in areas that are now prone to famine. a society that wants to develop welfare and justice and that wants more people to live in a healthy environment must ensure that there is economic growth. many mature industries are failing today. then we need new ones. biotechnology is one of them and one of the most promising. let us then, within the community, use common resources to continue to bring development together and forward. it is not enough to say we must succeed, we must also act. to act is to adopt damião's report. it's a positive action for a good future.
the submission of an opinion by the committee on budgetary control. madam president, i would like to thank you. i would like to say a few words about the opinion of the committee on budgetary control on this report. my committee and i are grateful that the rapporteur and the committee responsible have chosen to include so many of the points we have made. thank you so much for that! the european investment bank has for many years been one of the union's more anonymous power holders. at the same time, the eib has a very high degree of influence, owing to its extensive lending both to member states and to candidate countries. a broader political debate on the bank's direction is warranted, as is greater transparency in the bank's activities by the other eu institutions. the reason why the investment bank has been a rather anonymous power-holder in the eu is probably the special status of this body. it is a bank, and it is basically owned by the member states, while being part of the european union. we propose increased transparency, inter alia, by: we want to increase the court's access to information from the european investment bank. in the long term, this access to information should be similar to access to information from the central bank. this can be done through the tripartite agreement, which will be reviewed in the future. we would like to see more cooperation between our committee, the committee on budgetary control and the investment bank. the president of the bank, for example, could be welcomed to our meetings. we would also like to see more documents on the internal audit of the investment bank. we would like to see environmental considerations strengthened by the investment bank making the aarhus convention and its obligations part of its policy. it concerns public access to environmental information. we would also like to see an evaluation of the task given to us in gothenburg on the impact of climate change in a year's time. finally, i would just like to add that there is a need to evaluate the system of global loans, which is currently too vaguely defined. a thorough evaluation of the actual impact of the loans is needed.

mr president, i would like to thank you. i shall try to represent two people here today with this piece of art - myself and corbey, who was present at the conference in new delhi. that does not mean that i will speak with double tongues, but i will try to make the message as rigorous as possible. after reading the report from the commission and parliament on the new delhi conference, there is one of these countries reported extreme weather conditions at the new delhi conference. it was not just one country, but several, and they also spoke about the costs that this will entail. although we in the eu cannot take responsibility for all these environmental changes, we must nevertheless consider ourselves as one of the parties which have contributed more, together with the rest of the industrialized world, to creating the climate change that is now taking place. our role in helping the third world countries to protect themselves against climate change must be made clearer. we must of course also help them to find other ways of progressing in development than by trying out the way which we ourselves do not consider to be entirely successful. perhaps the most important thing is that the eu is still pushing for something to happen after 2012, that we are not giving up even though the outside world really wants something else. this is perhaps the most important message to come out of the new delhi conference.
mr president, i would like to thank you. once again we have an annual report, this time for 2001, which contains very serious criticism from the court of auditors on several points. as in the past, there are very concrete cases in a number of areas where eu funds are being used directly incorrectly, not least in the agricultural policy. the report also notes the huge surplus of eur 15 billion, or 16% of the budget. the most serious point in this annual report is undoubtedly the general criticism of the reliability of the eu's accounting system and financial management. both in the report and when it was presented to the committee, this criticism was very clear. at the presentation to the committee, the court of auditors representatives said that only 5% of the payments could actually be guaranteed to be correct because of the way the system works. this is something very big and very serious. besides, the changes seem to be slow. the court of auditors' report states that in may this year the implementation of the action plan to bring about improvements had not even begun. despite this serious criticism of the accounting system, the auditors' statement nevertheless chooses to say that the transactions were made correctly. i mean, there's a big contradiction in this. it is, in principle, impossible to claim both of these things at the same time. you cannot both demonstrate an untraceable accounting system and guarantee that everything is done correctly. i question whether there is any basis for this statement of assurance. we all know the strong criticism of the accounts by the former head of the area, marta andreasen. she also refused to sign the accounts for the year 2001. i would like to ask the court of auditors if it understands this, in view of the criticism which the court of auditors itself has made. isn't that a pretty reasonable position not to sign? another interesting point made by marta andreasen is that it is possible to make rapid improvements without new investment in the commission. it would be interesting to hear the court's opinion on this too. i am a little surprised, by the way, that this debate rejects the criticism of mr andreasen. it may be wrong, but why is it not being criticized? why do the other political groups in this parliament not want a hearing to be held? why is the commission putting a muzzle on andreasen when meps invite her to help us with our audit work? it doesn't look very nice.
mr president, i would like to thank you. i am going to raise the great risk that i believe the copenhagen council meeting may pose to the eu as a peace project. we are all talking about the unification of the european union. we all talk about the historic decision in copenhagen. but can the eu as a peace project be demolished in copenhagen? many members will probably feel that this is not a relevant question, but i think it is. the eu has been extremely successful in bringing people and states closer together in the european union. however, we now have a candidate country which is first in line to join the european union, namely cyprus. after the fall of the berlin wall, nicosia is the divided capital of europe. after the reunification of germany, cyprus is the divided country in europe. the european parliament has clearly stated its desire for the withdrawal of the turkish occupation forces from northern cyprus. at this stage, discussions are taking place at a very high level in the council on the issue of giving turkey a date for a meeting to set a new date for accession negotiations for that country. i therefore address the danish presidency in particular: how will the public, how will the cypriots, and how will we all together, view the situation when a country like turkey is given a date to open accession negotiations while 30,000 turkish troops remain in northern cyprus? is the eu a peace project? i really want to dwell on this subject and wonder if we can conceive of the idea of a country negotiating with the european union and at the same time having evidence of occupying 37% of another member state? this has to be settled in copenhagen! if it is conceivable that the eu peace project would allow turkey to enter into negotiations without the cyprus conflict being resolved, then this means that a member state could be occupied by a country seeking membership of the union and actually negotiating with the commission. i want this matter settled. let us not be blinded by the turkish charm offensive. let us look at the hard facts that say that one country, cyprus, is on its way to the union, and another country, occupying 37 per cent of that country, wants to start negotiations. does that make sense? is the eu a peace project?
the committee on the environment, public health and consumer protection has tabled an opinion on the proposal for a council directive amending directive 76/769/eec on the approximation of the laws of the member states relating to the labelling, presentation and advertising of foodstuffs. mr president, i would like to thank you. i would like to commend commissioner fischler for his insightful remarks at the beginning of the debate. we know that the environmental situation is facing a catastrophe in terms of fisheries and sustainability. it is not just cod and whitefish that are concerned, but all scientists are concerned that we are depleting most of our fish species. we must remember that without fish there are no fishermen, there is no economy, and there is no fisheries policy. what we are also losing is a very important coastal and fishing culture in almost all european countries, which is perhaps the worst disaster for us. however, we cannot continue to fish in this way. we have a responsibility not only to those now living but also to the future of our children and grandchildren. the committee on the environment also took up the fact that we rich europeans buy fishing rights from poorer countries for substantial sums of money but for very little money. what are we doing there? what sustainability plans do we have? what readiness is there to help the poor coastal fishermen on the shores of africa? do we have the right to behave as colonially as we do in the case of fisheries agreements with the third world? there are some beautiful words on paper, but what is reality? now we've fished ourselves into disaster. let us not, for god's sake, fish the poor to the brink of disaster!
mr president, i would like to thank you. my remarks also concern the crisis in the cod fishery. the international council for the exploration of the sea has found that cod stocks in the north sea, the irish sea and skagerrak are threatened with total collapse. they're calling for a full stop. do we listen to their advice and suggestions? no, we're just trying to find every excuse to avoid taking responsibility. i find it outrageous to hear members in this house, particularly the chairman of the committee on fisheries, scold the commission when it is doing its best to take its responsibilities. the ices opinion on the baltic sea is perhaps a little more difficult to interpret, but it seems to be based on the most optimistic view, particularly as regards the blackfish. however, sweden has decided to apply a unilateral moratorium on cod fishing. we live by the precautionary principle. if we do not, it could mean the future of the fishing industry being completely destroyed. my question to the commission is this: does the commission fully support the swedish unilateral fishing ban? can commissioner fischler repeat what he said when he was in sweden last week, namely that compensation for fishermen is also supported?

madam president, i would like to thank you. we in the christian-democratic group support the proposal of mr karas and the commission. as regards economic and employment policies in the european union, we are approaching the moment of truth. the demands and objectives of lisbon and gothenburg must now be realised. simplifications are more important than streamlining. it is important to increase participation, openness and transparency. it is also important for us christian democrats to state that we are in favour of a market economy in europe that is socially, ecologically and ethically based. it should be human-friendly. it's supposed to be family friendly. it must allow for personal responsibility, creativity but also for security and initiative. open coordination does not mean uniformity, but it allows member states to work closely together to find the best solutions for each country. it is important to combine the environmental objective of gothenburg with the requirement of economic growth and social cohesion in order to achieve the three dimensions. we regret that the commission has not included this in its communication. as christian democrats, let us also stress that trust and ethical and moral values are fundamental to social and economic development. if there is no trust and no ethical and moral basis in the economy, it cannot develop. the european parliament must be given greater involvement without requiring a decision-making power. it is a question of giving the european parliament not only the right to be present but also time. in conclusion, i would like to say that it is important that the candidate countries quickly join this work and that the regional and local authorities also play an active role.

the commission is not prepared to accept this. i am old enough to remember the violent debate on irradiated food that raged across europe 15 or 20 years ago. it was very similar to the gmo debate that we have today. it is a fact that the consensus of scientific opinion, who and fao, says that radiation itself is completely harmless. therefore, recitals i and j of this report are quite misleading. the technology is probably good for those parts of the world where food security simply means having something to eat. having said that, however, i would also like to say emphatically that in today's europe there is no reason at all to irradiate anything other than spices. there are good reasons for doing so, however, since other methods of sterilizing spices are even more unsuitable from a health and environmental standpoint. otherwise, it is unnecessary to use this technology in a continent and in a system where we are working very hard to create a high level of safety throughout the food chain. if we maintain a high enough level of hygiene, apply good production practices and keep the refrigeration and freezing chain intact, we have no need for a technology which can - i am not saying it always does - be misused to hide a lack of hygiene or to hide flaws in the refrigeration chain, etc. it's a little too hard to control this. of course, we can say that this treatment is ok, if it is only indicated in the form of a label, but i think the risks of misuse of this technology are too great. i don't think it's dangerous, but i think it's highly inappropriate for us.
mr president, i would like to thank you. like the previous speaker, i do not think there are any valid arguments for allowing the irradiation of food other than spices. instead, in several member states, controls on the irradiation of foodstuffs which are not to be irradiated should be tightened up. we want to make the report more precise by adopting amendments nos 2 and 3, and thus make this parliament's position. there is a clear risk that irradiation is being used to conceal poor hygienic handling or to conceal the fact that food is too old to be sold. we have no reason to allow methods that enable this. it is also a fact that if food is irradiated to preserve it for a longer period of time, it risks losing its nutritional value and thus becoming a less nutritious food for the consumer. i mean, consumers are then somehow tricked into buying something that is not of the same quality. this is partly also a question of what kind of agricultural and food policy we should have. do we want to have extreme scale, with long shelf life and very long transportation? or should we build an agricultural and food policy based on most being produced locally and delivered and consumed within a reasonable time? we are in favour of the latter!
in writing. (sv) we voted against the report in order not to omit something which we consider to be of fundamental importance. paragraph 5 (2) provided that the procedure for ratifying a future constitution should be designed in such a way that a 'small minority' of member states could not oppose ratification. this is unacceptable, because this right of veto is one of the institutional cornerstones of the eu. we think it would be very sad if the european parliament were to send the message to the assembly and the intergovernmental conference that, as suggested in the report in its original form, a minority of one third of the eu member states could be ignored. we would also like to point out that there can be no requirement that any future constitutional proposals by an assembly cannot be amended by the european council without the approval of the assembly. the democratic legitimacy of the assembly in such a case cannot be considered superior to that of governments. we would like to stress that, although we are in principle in favour of extending the codecision procedure and qualified majority voting to all legislation, we believe that unanimity may be required in the areas of foreign and security policy. we would also like to point out that the co-decision procedure under article 139 must not imply any restriction on the ability of the social partners to reach agreements autonomously. the co-decision procedure should not lead to a change in the existing procedure, in such a way as to examine the substance of the agreements, which is incompatible with the role of the legislative authority.
in writing. (sv) one of the main tasks of the assembly is to make proposals for simplifying and clarifying the legal instruments and regulations applicable in the eu, and to clarify which competences have been transferred by the member states to the eu. the assembly's efforts to shape a single decision-making structure based on codecision and the community model in a combined 'constitutional treaty' involve important changes aimed at democratizing the eu decision-making process and simplifying it in order to make it more understandable. the report makes sound proposals for the adoption of new terminology to clarify the way the various eu legal instruments work and to facilitate access to the decision-making process. the report makes it clear that the eu can only act in areas where member states have delegated corresponding competences to it. the areas in which member states choose to delegate their competences are characterised by the clear added value of cooperation over unilateral action by member states, in particular as regards the internal market and the joint fight against serious cross-border crime. the report is not clear as to whether the change of circumstances should be based solely on the outcome of the convention and whether future assemblies should approve any changes suggested by the intergovernmental conferences on the proposals of the assemblies. the obligation on the part of future intergovernmental conferences to comply with the decisions of the convention would radically alter the relationship between the eu and the member states. the requirement of unanimity in the event of a change of circumstances must be maintained. the sanctions procedures must be respected and continue to operate as they currently do. it is also unclear whether the proposed laws, which are complementary to the constitution and which are based on qualified majority voting, will determine how the eu is financed in the future. eu funding must be decided unanimously. the swedish moderates are opposed to proposals which could lead to eu funding from eu-wide taxation.

the importance of our being able to show unity in the face of enlargement, have contributed to a substantive and constructive process. i would like to thank the danish presidency and the danish minister for finance, mr thor pedersen, whom i hope we will see here on thursday. he has helped to lead the council members to a very constructive settlement. i would also like to thank the commission and mr schreyer, my co-rapporteurs, mr per stenmarck and mr joan colom i naval, and the budget rapporteurs from the other committees who have contributed very constructively to this process. last but not least, i would like to thank my colleagues on the committee on budgets, the excellent secretariat of the committee and, not least, its excellent chairman, mr terence wynn, who has always been a great support in the work. when this process began a little over a year ago, one of my objectives was to develop the procedure in parliament itself, and i think we have made some progress. we have established a dialogue on objectives and guidelines at an early stage. we have worked closely with the other specialised committees to involve them actively in the budgetary process. we have developed the new budget debate in september, something which will certainly be able to develop further next year. this has worked well. unfortunately, i have to be critical, too. it is not our work on the budget, but the eu budgetary system. in my view, it is the longterm budgetary plan and the interinstitutional agreement that set sectoral budget ceilings for seven years at a time. it's a completely unreasonable model. such a long-term and detailed forecasting is not possible in a modern budgetary system. it's more like the soviets. i certainly accept budgetary discipline. i am a big supporter of budgetary ceilings, but this model is far too rigid. as long as it rules us, we will all be constantly looking for loopholes out of the straitjacket of financial perspective. since the introduction of the budget in 1999, we have used the flexibility mechanism to a greater or lesser extent each year. we have also developed increasingly creative new methods of flexibility. we have a reserve of emergency aid, which we are using to an ever-increasing extent. we have added the new solidarity fund, which is also an instrument of flexibility. this year we ourselves have introduced a systematic use of front loading, where we find unused resources in the current year's budget to advance expenditure for the next year and thereby create space. this creativity has been very helpful, but it's not very open or transparent. it's not a good budgeting methodology for the long term. i therefore urge our colleagues in the convention to give serious thought to how we can increase parliament's influence on the budget as a whole and in the long term in future treaties, while at the same time creating a less rigid and anti-development model of budgetary ceilings. i am also critical of the implementation of the budget, although we are seeing improvements in some areas, for example in aid policy. the fact that in 2001 we had to return 15% of the member states' contributions to the eu because we were unable to implement what we had decided in the budget is of course unreasonable. in the coming years, if the rate of implementation remains low, we risk that regions which have received promises of support and candidate countries which have received generous promises of pre-accession aid will rightly protest when the promises are not implemented. on the other hand, if the rate of implementation improves, we risk having major problems in keeping the budget in the coming years if the mountains of payments we are rolling forward are to be implemented while enlargement is taking up large new resources. despite these shortcomings, i think we have much to be happy about. let me just mention a few of the most important areas. this is of course the case with enlargement. the front-loading model we have developed has enabled us to provide the commission with the resources it needs, including the 500 new posts required for enlargement. i am sure that my colleague, mr stenmarck, will speak more about this in relation to the budget for parliament and the other institutions. however, enlargement has also had an impact in other areas. the new large-scale information resources will apply to enlargement. a range of eu-supported agencies are now putting those resources to work on integrating the new countries. it is a question of social dialogue, youth cooperation, the joint fight against crime, etc. we have also launched a new pilot project for cooperation between small businesses, both with candidate countries and with the countries along the new external border of the union - russia, the balkans, north africa, etc. i think this is the most important thing we have achieved. let me also mention the eu's efforts to combat poverty and disease in developing countries. although i am personally disappointed that we have not brought about more clear changes in agricultural policy, because that would probably be the most important thing we could do in the fight against poverty, i am nevertheless glad that we can do other good things. i know there is some criticism of the fact that we have reduced the allocation for food aid and humanitarian aid. however, we have done this in order to provide early aid to the starving horn of africa and to free up resources for afghanistan next year. this has been done only on condition that we and the council pledge to provide the additional resources for this which may be needed next year. we have also been criticised for the way we have handled the eu contribution to the global health fund. i think that criticism is unfair. we have got the council to more than double its proposal from its first reading, from 20 million to 42 million. we have also been given the promise by the council to try to raise the equivalent amount from the european development fund. finally, we have a common commitment to contribute, under all circumstances, the resources needed to enable the eu to retain its seat on the fund's board of directors. i don't think it's fair to ask for more than that. finally, we have also reached a very good agreement on the future role of parliament in foreign and security policy, and i think we are all satisfied with that. ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, this budget will not be made historic by some dramatic conflicts or by some great revolutionary new budgetary initiative. but it will be historic, because it is the last budget with the old budget nomenclature, and next year we will all have to learn activity-based budgeting. it will also be historic because it is the last budget to deal with an eu of 15 member states. i am particularly pleased that we have actually reached the solutions needed to prepare for enlargement by means of such broad political consensus. i think this is the biggest success of the year. (applause) i would like to thank you.
the rapporteur. mr president, i would like to thank you. we are now at the end of a comprehensive process to produce a budget for the coming financial year, and i would like to thank everyone who has been involved in this work. this is true of course of the committee on budgets and not least of its chairman, mr terence wynn, and of my swedish colleague, mr göran färm, but it is particularly true of the very competent office of the committee on budgets. i would also like to express my sincere thanks to both the commission and the council for their constructive cooperation. i would also like to thank the danish presidency in particular for its active role in ensuring that the two budgetary authorities of the eu could reach a joint agreement on the category 5 framework at a conciliation meeting on 19 july this year, which i believe was very important. throughout the current year, the administrative budget of the european union, what we call category 5, has been one of the real problem children of the budget. we started the year with a significant projected deficit in the 2003 budget. in various stages these have been then taken down, giving us a manageable surplus and a margin which i consider to be fully acceptable for the next financial year. within this budget we have also succeeded in implementing the priorities we set when the guidelines were drawn up at the beginning of the year. the first and obvious priority was enlargement. we know from the decisions that have been taken since then that the member states, not least the positive decision at the copenhagen summit, are living up to their commitments with regard to the ten new member states. if the agreement is signed in march, we will be able to have 147 observers from all these countries on the ground as early as april. the necessary decisions will be ready and implemented by then. it has also been a matter of giving each institution the opportunity to complete its preparations for enlargement. this question takes on added significance now that we see the happy decision from copenhagen. mr president, i would like to thank you. i would like to thank all the institutions for their active participation in this work. this is particularly true of the great loyalty to enlargement and the european project that everyone showed in the context of the operation front loading that we have carried out together. the second priority was to implement the necessary reforms. with or without enlargement, these became necessary, not least for the european parliament. it was mainly about such key areas where every parliament, whether national or european, must be strong. for the european parliament, it was a matter of strengthening the budgetary structure, and this does not primarily concern the committee on budgets, but rather the other committees in their extremely important budgetary work. the legislative function of the parliament must also be strengthened. the european parliament has acquired considerable legislative powers in a very short time, which means that its capacity in this area must also be strengthened. these are key areas for the european parliament, and it is here that we must be particularly strong. the third priority is to deal with all of this. the preparations for enlargement and the necessary reforms must be carried out within the budgetary limits. this we have succeeded in doing. this means that we are now entering a new financial year with a rather large margin, which will hopefully amount to just over 20 million. this is subject to parliament's approval of the oral amendment which i know my colleague, mr göran färm, will be presenting and to which i therefore ask to be allowed to subscribe. let me say that i am aware that the council would like an even larger margin. i have a great deal of sympathy for this, and i promised at the conciliation to see what i could do to achieve an even greater margin. i have since gone over the whole budget again, and we have now come this far, but unfortunately no further. mr president, i would like to thank you. let me conclude by saying that this is the last budget that the european parliament will adopt before the enlargement of the union to ten new member states. it has been important to carry out all this in a way that enlargement preparations can be managed in all the institutions. it is my judgment that this can happen. it was also important to take a number of decisions during the year to deal with long-term problems in category 5. i believe that we have done this too, but it is important that this work be followed up. further decisions in this direction will be necessary in the coming financial year. (applause) i would like to thank you.
madam president, i would like to thank you. ries has given an excellent account of the negotiations on the settlement and the problems that have arisen in connection with it. in the parliament's delegation we can be very pleased that we were able to reach an agreement which also included the octs, which of course means that a ban can be introduced more quickly. i do, however, see it as a failure for the protection of the environment and health in the eu that the commission and the council did not follow parliament's proposal to include decabde in the ban. at least 75-80 per cent of all uses of brominated flame retardants involve deca and the available risk assessment shows that decabde should have been banned immediately. further waiting entails further risks to public health. these substances are long-lived and bioaccumulative. they're stored in living organisms, and they cause behavioral disturbances in experimental animals. deca can also be converted into pentabde and octabde, which is exactly what the council wants to ban. it can also be converted to brominated dioxins. the scientific evidence is so worrying for health and the environment that the european chemicals agency recommended that member states make a political decision to use the precautionary principle. the competent authority of the member states considered in june 2002 that it was necessary to take action immediately, without delay. in conclusion, i must say that the result is a failure for the eu's environmental and health policy. i therefore believe that mr ries understands that my group will be abstaining in the final vote.
madam president, i would like to thank you. the word 'historical' has been used very frequently in the copenhagen reviews. it's starting to feel a little drained, but there's no better word. it is a historic decision that ten countries, former communist dictatorships, are now welcomed into the community and the warmth! finally, the continent and its citizens are united! it was also historic that the door was opened so clearly to bulgaria and romania, and it was also historic that the signal was sent to turkey. the eu is not a christian club. it is a union of values, defending democracy and human rights. i am sure that all summits are a team effort, but there are some appalling examples of this teamwork having failed and the smoke is in the air. it's been felt far beyond the confines of the facility. there was certainly a certain drama in copenhagen; it can never be too much of a set-up from the start. despite this, and especially despite the concessions in agricultural policy, the liberals hope that the reforms will continue. the will was strong. everyone was open to compromise, but the danes should have the credit for the result. your goal, from copenhagen to copenhagen, has been crystal clear all the way. even though some doubted, you never did, rasmussen and haarder but you were always crystal clear. when others, like hamlet at helsingør castle, were quelling his "to be or not to be" monologue, you had the course clearly set. your efforts will go down in history. as a liberal, i am very proud that it is a liberal government that has achieved this. however, political victories can be enjoyed only for brief moments. we'll be back to work after christmas. the eu must be reformed. it requires strong leadership and political courage to dare to implement some of the institutional reforms required. the eu must become more efficient, which requires more majority decisions, not least in agricultural policy. the eu must become more democratic. we need a common constitution, a common vision and a common leadership. referendums will be held. we must all show that we welcome the new countries. they will not become b-members, but the new countries will be as welcome as we are. the greek presidency has a great responsibility. finally, madam president, we must also tackle the question of how big the eu can really be. we are eagerly awaiting the commission's report on the strategy for the future neighbourhood.
mr president, i would like to thank you. after all this praise, one hardly dares to utter a single word of criticism against denmark, especially as a swede. you could be suspected of being jealous! the swedish presidency ended in a bloody police fiasco, while the danish peace summit may end up in the history books. yet there is some legitimate criticism. denmark has failed to prove that the eu is a project for the people and not just for the elites. the disastrous agricultural policy of the european union, which is fertilising counts and barons but bankrupting small farmers in eastern europe, has not been dismantled. there has been no concern, for example in estonia, that the eu bureaucracy will crush the newfound self-determination. it has not been explained why poland is being forced to build a new iron curtain to the east; titley, the iron curtain is not disappearing, it is merely being moved! it has not been explained in a comprehensible way why the free movement of capital should apply from day one but that of people much later. the enlargement process has thus once again become a unilateral annexation, with brussels dictating and the candidate countries submitting. denmark, which has its own exceptions, should understand that an enlarged union cannot be uniform, because it would then become totalitarian. it must be diverse if it is to be democratic. the next step is to ensure that the referendums in the candidate countries are democratic. the first thing to do is to ensure that we do not decide on the eu constitution before the new member states have joined. if the eu were to decide on a constitution, right under the noses of the new eu members, then the arrogance of power has triumphed and democracy has lost. then denmark's success at the copenhagen summit will be the calm before the storm.
mr president, i would like to thank you. today, denmark and bertel haarder have received well-deserved praise. i agree with that. however, i am less satisfied with the eu's refugee and immigration policy. the proposals are usually about control and repression. the big picture is easily lost. what does it take? it is that the world is fighting poverty and that we have more aid, more trade and more democracy. haarder knows that. we need a humane asylum policy based on respect and dignity. all people have the right to seek asylum within the eu. the european union is also committed to the development of a common foreign and security policy, which will be based on the principles of the treaty of rome and the treaty of rome. opening the eu to labour immigration would relieve the pressure on asylum policy and at the same time give us a chance to cope with the growing population problems. the need for a common eu asylum policy is illustrated by the danish example. when denmark tightens, the refugees come to sweden. a humanist europe must do better than that. this requires a vote in this house, which mr haarder knows better than anyone.

rapporteur. – mr president, i have some technical changes to announce before the vote. earlier this week i gave many well-deserved thanks to the commission and mrs schreyer, my fellow rapporteur; my colleagues in the committee on budgets and our dear chairman, mr wynn et al. however, as the danish finance minister was not present at the time, let me repeat my sincere thanks to the very constructive and efficient danish presidency and in particular the finance minister, mr pedersen. he has managed to unite all the member states and find solutions with us to very difficult issues. he has paved the way for the very broadly accepted solutions that form the basis for this, hopefully, short and uncomplicated vote today. i thank the danish presidency very much. (applause)let me also thank the extremely efficient and competent secretariat of the committee on budgets, who worked extremely well under extreme pressure, but also in a very nice and friendly atmosphere. finally, i warmly thank the sittings services who have always handled complicated issues on voting lists etc. in a very correct and efficient manner. (applause)now i turn to the formalities. before the vote on the budgetary amendments starts, i must make a statement about some technical adjustments, some of which need to be put to the vote. first, a purely technical adjustment: amendment 1 on budget lines b1-406 and b1-4081 – improving the processing and marketing of agricultural products – has been withdrawn, because it is covered by letter of amendment no 3 to the draft budget, which we have already voted on. a vote is necessary to confirm the following corrections. first, amendment 25 on budget line b2-5120 – farm accountancy data network (fadn) – the figures should be modified as follows: an addition of eur 1 391 000 in commitments and of eur 1 383 000 in payments, giving the final figures of eur 11 337 000 in commitments and eur 11 329 000 in payments. secondly, amendment 238 on budget line b2-517 on plant and animal genetic resources. this should be withdrawn because it has also been covered by letter of amendment no 3. thirdly, amendment 246 on budget line b3-3062 on prince. the following five words should be deleted in the remark: '... of the national public sectors'. lastly, in amendment 168 on budget line a-200 – rent and ground rent – the figures should be modified as follows: add eur 3 409 073, giving a final amount of eur 132 590 904. i suggest that we vote on these corrections en bloc before we start voting on the amendments.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the resolution. we, the swedish christian democrats, are glad to see that the division of europe is now a thing of the past. world war ii was thus brought to a permanent end. cooperation within the eu is no longer limited to western europe, but encompasses almost all of europe. the eu as a peace project, however, risks being seriously jeopardised if the council sets a date for accession negotiations with turkey as long as it maintains the military occupation, which began in 1974, of 37 per cent of the territory of the republic of cyprus, a future eu member state. i therefore find it deeply regrettable that amendment no 13, which recalls this state of affairs, has not been adopted. amendment no 10 by the christian-democratic group of the epp-ed gave some leeway to the revision of the aid strategy for turkey on the basis of a special partnership. it is a pity that this amendment, which was an alternative and complement to turkey's european ambitions, was rejected by 201 votes to 238 with 16 abstentions.

- the next item is the joint debate on the following seven motions for resolutions on hong kong: - b5-0643/2002 by mr andreasen, mr plooij-van gorsel, mr van den bos and mr watson, on behalf of the eldr group, on the situation in hong kong; - b5-0645/2002 by mr sjöstedt, mr eriksson, mr frahm and mr di lello finuoli, on behalf of the gue/ngl group, on the constitution of hong kong; - b5-0649/2002 by mr gahrton and mr wuori, on behalf of the green group in the european parliament, on article 23 of the constitution of hong kong; - b5-0650/2002 by mr van den berg, on behalf of the pse group, on article 23 of the constitution of hong kong; - b5-0653/2002 by mr belder, on behalf of the edd group, on article 23 of the constitution of hong kong; - b5-0655/2002 by mr cushnahan, mr posselt and mr sacrédeus, on behalf of the group of the european people's party, on article 23 of the constitution of hong kong; - b5-0657/2002 by mr muscardini, mr ribeiro e castro and mr queiró, on behalf of the uen group, on article 23 of the constitution of hong kong.
madam president, i would like to thank you. commissioner, what is it? the eldr group is very concerned about the proposed amendment of article 23 of the hong kong constitution. in talks with journalists, politicians, various religious groups, representatives of human rights organisations and organisations such as falun gong, they all express very great concern at this planned change. hong kong's strength is that it has managed to preserve the principle of one country, two systems. i fear, and many fear with me, that this change will seriously undermine that principle. this would, of course, be totally unacceptable. the fact that china is pushing for this change is not reassuring. we know that china often exerts pressure on hong kong to adopt legislation that is in china. i am afraid that the interpretation of article 23 could lead to the disappearance of fundamental freedoms such as academic freedom, freedom of expression, freedom of association and freedom of religion. if a group like falun gong, which is banned in china, were also banned in hong kong, it would be a serious violation of the principle: one country, two systems. i'm not the only one who's worried. the huge demonstration this weekend, in which thousands of hong kong citizens protested against the change, shows that the hong kong authorities have failed to reassure their own citizens, and therefore they are not reassuring us either. if this is just a technical change, as hong kong claims, then we must be very clear on this point. i'm afraid it's not that way. i hope that the commission will be monitoring this matter very closely. i would also appeal to the hong kong authorities to extend the deadline and to be very vigilant about these changes.
madam president, i would like to thank you. in september, the hong kong government introduced a draft of a new law that threatens freedom of speech, press and religion in the region. this law, known as article 23, aims to 'protect hong kong from external and domestic threats', but at the same time it harmonises hong kong's legislation with china's. thus, organizations and movements that are banned in communist china can also be banned in hong kong. the falun gong movement, as well as christian churches not approved by the chinese government, would be given the same illegal status in hong kong as it has had in china since 1999. the law would increase the authority of the authorities to control and thus censor everything printed in newspapers, other media and other published material. any material deemed harmful to the region and china as a whole would thus be censored and criminal to possess. the vote is scheduled to take place in hong kong on christmas eve. honor the holiday of christmas by withdrawing article 23!
the commission is not prepared to accept the amendments tabled by the committee on budgets. the commission has followed closely the public debate on the proposal adopted by the hong kong government on 24 september to implement article 23 of the basic law of hong kong. it is aware of the great importance and political sensitivity of this initiative, also in the context of developments in hong kong and china as a whole. as this proposal by the territorial government is very general in nature, a careful assessment of the draft law will only be possible once it has been submitted to the legislative council of hong kong. this is expected to be the case in february 2003. the commission is building on the fact that a comprehensive consultation process is now taking place, which will later be extended to the detailed legislation once it is submitted to the legislative council. the commission considers it essential that the views of the different groups expressed in the course of this consultation process be fully taken into account in the drafting of future legislation. furthermore, the future legislation and its implementation must not undermine hong kong's respect for the rule of law and respect for human rights and fundamental freedoms, in accordance with its 'one country, two systems' principle. finally, the hong kong basic law requires that the proposed legislation be compatible with the un conventions on civil and political rights and economic, social and cultural rights. the european union, through its ambassadors, is closely following developments in this regard. on the basis of its recommendations, it intends to submit to the hong kong sar government, as soon as possible and in any event before the end of the consultations on 24 december, a declaration at the highest possible level underlining the commitment of the european union to the above principles. (applause from the centre)
- thank you, commissioner. the joint discussion is closed. the vote will take place at 5.30 p.m.
- doc. b5-0644/2002 by mr morgantini, mr boudjenah, mr herman schmid, mr seppänen, mr cossutta and mr miranda da silva, on behalf of the gue/ngl group, on the destruction of cultural heritage in hebron; - b5-0647/2002 by mr lagendijk, mr boumediene-thiery, mr dhaene, mr gahrton and mr isler-beguin, on behalf of the green group in the european parliament, on the destruction of cultural heritage in hebron; - b5-0652/2002 by mr trentin and mr van den berg, on behalf of the pse group, on the destruction of cultural heritage in hebron; - b5-0654/2002 by mr perry and mr morillon, on behalf of the ppe group, on the destruction of cultural heritage in hebron.
the commission is not prepared to accept the amendments tabled by the committee on budgets. the commission is deeply concerned about the planned demolition of the homes of palestinian families in the old city of hebron. these buildings are not only of cultural and historical importance, but the planned action will also further increase the suffering of innocent palestinian civilians. the european union condemned the attack by palestinian militants on 15 november, which killed 12 israeli soldiers. the commission cannot see how the planned measures would improve security in hebron. the measures will further exacerbate the already extremely tense situation in hebron. the commission would point out that the destruction of historic palestinian houses is in breach of the hebron protocol agreed between israel and the palestinian authority in 1997. it commits both parties to preserve and protect the historic character of the city so that it is not damaged or altered in any part of the city. the commission would also like to draw parliament's attention to the recent conclusions of the copenhagen european council. the european council is alarmed by the expansion of settlements and the associated construction. he urged israel to reverse its settlement policy, to stop building settlements and to stop seizing more land.

- the joint debate on the following six motions for resolutions on tibet: - b5-0646/2002 by mr sjöstedt, mr eriksson, mr frahm and mr di lello finuoli, on behalf of the gue/ngl group, on the human rights situation in tibet; - b5-0648/2002 by mr messner, mr gahrton, mr maes and mr wuori, on behalf of the green group in the european parliament, on the human rights situation in tibet; - b5-0651/2002 by mr van den berg, on behalf of the group of the european people's party, on the situation of the rights of tibetans in sichuan province; - b5-0656/2002 by mr mann and mr posselt, on behalf of the group of the european people's party, on the human rights situation in tibet; - b5-0658/2002 by mrs angelilli, on behalf of the uen group, on the human rights situation in tibet.
madam president, i would like to thank you. commissioner, what is it? we have discussed tibet many times in this house. this is certainly not the last. solidarity with the tibetan people is very strong in the european parliament. the tibetan people have been subjected to terrible treatment by beijing for many years. this is still happening. but perhaps there is a small, small light in the darkness because there has been a cautious dialogue between beijing and representatives of the dalai lama. the fact that two people like ngawang sangdrol and jigme sangpo, two very well known prisoners of conscience and repeatedly brought to the attention of this chamber, have now been released from their long-term captivity is also very positive. there are many more prisoners, however. i do not believe that these developments indicate any fundamental change in policy on the part of china. i wish i could. i do not think that is the case. at least it's a step along the way. the fact that the two tibetan buddhists, tenzin delek and lobsang dhondup, have now been sentenced to death is all the more worrying. they were in the wrong place at the wrong time and are now branded as terrorists. they have not had a fair trial, no evidence has been presented, they have been held in total isolation for eight months and now they are to be executed. the eldr group calls for a fair and just legal process and for treatment in accordance with international conventions. as always, we condemn the death penalty wherever it occurs. unfortunately, china's contribution to the international fight against terrorism seems to have so far been limited to labelling opposition dissidents and various minorities as terrorists, thus providing beijing with an alibi for suppressing them. that's not how it's going to be. the fight against terrorism must always be combined with the rule of law. we will continue to fight for the rights of tibet, continue to condemn all human rights violations in china and hope that the commission will intensify the human rights dialogue with china.
the commission is not prepared to accept the amendments tabled by the committee on budgets. the commission welcomes the initiative of parliament to table a resolution on the human rights situation in tibet. we are extremely concerned about the death sentences handed down on 2 december to lobsang dhondup and tenzin delek. we recall that mr delek was on the list of individual cases handed over to the chinese authorities as part of the eu-china human rights dialogue. the commission is also concerned that these two death sentences are the first to be imposed on tibetans for alleged political offences in several years. the commission takes this opportunity to inform the european parliament that on 13 december the eu troika made a diplomatic approach to the chinese authorities, requesting information on the two cases and in particular on the evidence used, and calling for the reopening of the proceedings in accordance with international fair trial rules. in addition, the troika reaffirmed the eu's general opposition to the death penalty and its application in china, with reference to the ongoing campaign of so-called 'hard-line' measures. thanks to the prompt intervention of the european union and pressure from the usa and australia, the executions have not been carried out and the two convicts have appealed. consequently, the execution cannot take place until the people's court has reviewed the case. although we believe that the imminent threat of execution has been averted, the commission will continue to follow this case with the utmost attention, in particular as regards the provision of solid evidence and guarantees of a fair trial. the european union continues to put pressure on china to ensure the protection of the cultural, linguistic and religious identity of the tibetan people. it has also repeatedly called for the establishment of direct dialogue between beijing and the dalai lama, as this is likely to be the only way to ensure a peaceful and lasting settlement of the tibetan issue. in this context, the commission is encouraged by the positive message conveyed by the two envoys of the dalai lama during their visit to china in september 2002. we hope that this was a first step towards establishing a more regular dialogue between beijing and the dalai lama. the commission will continue to take only those cooperation measures in tibet which directly benefit the tibetan people. - we're taking note of that. the vote is closed. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'competent authority' to cover the whole of the european union.
in writing. (sv) the the swedish moderate party delegation in the european parliament voted against the report by mrs krazza-tsagaropoulou on the commission's sixth annual report on equal opportunities in the european union for 2001. we believe that equality derives from the equal value of every human being and from the right of every individual to respect for himself and his choices in his personal, family and professional life. it is a matter of justice that no one should be discriminated against on the grounds of sex or any other grounds. any form of oppression, discrimination or stereotyping must be combated and the opportunity for every person to make free choices promoted. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. we oppose the parts of the report which advocate the adoption of quotas and what is defined as 'positive discrimination'. it is not the job of politicians to determine how private sector employers make hiring decisions in the light of these concerns. mathematical redistribution does not imply justice and does not lead to real equality. however, selection procedures for recruitment and entry to higher education should ensure, as far as possible, gender neutrality. in addition, we believe that the ongoing work on equality is first and foremost about changing attitudes in the long term and we would like to emphasize the importance of the school in changing attitudes in this direction. we believe that the committee on employment and social affairs should be responsible for these matters, which are currently being dealt with by the committee on women's rights and equal opportunities.
- thank you all and a merry christmas and a happy new year. membership of political groups - membership of committees/authorisation to draw up own-initiative reports - statements entered in the register (rule 51) - transmission of texts adopted at this part-session - calendar of next part-sessions: see minutes.
- i declare the session of the european parliament adjourned. (the sitting was closed at 5.25 p.m.)
